Signalons ici que la réalisation des principes d’interpolation (isogénique, de Langlands, classique, quantique et colorée) ne peut pas être réalisée directement à partir des algèbres GQE. Rappelons en effet que le paramètre formel
h
ne peut pas être spécialisé à une valeur autre que zéro en l’absente de certaines précautions, rendant difficile à première vue toute interpolation. Le relais est à cette fin transmis aux algèbres de
Kac-Moody
colorées.

6.15
Contribution au programme de Frenkel-Hernandez

La réalisation du principe d’interpolation de Langlands quantique, cas particulier du théorème
36
en considérant l’
isogénie de Langlands
, apporte une solution à une extension de la conjecture de Frenkel-Hernandez : on rappelle en effet qu’un quotient de l’algèbre de Frenkel-Hernandez
{U_{\!\!\>q,t}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})
est contenu dans une algèbre de Kac-Moody colorée (voir le théorème
19
). Le résultat suivant affirme ainsi que la conjecture GQE implique la conjecture de Frenkel-Hernandez.

On rappelle que
\varepsilon
désigne une racine primitive de l’unité d’ordre
2d
.

Théorème 38
.

Pour toute représentation
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
dans la catégorie
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!q}(\mathfrak{g})}
, il existe une représentation
{V_{\!\!\;q,t}}
de
{U_{\!\!\>q,t}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})
vérifiant les deux points suivants.

•

L’action de
X^{\pm}_{i}
sur
{V_{\!\!\;q,t}}
peut être spécialisée à
{t\!=\!1}
et est alors égale à l’action de
X_{i}^{\pm}

sur
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
.

•

L’action de
(X^{\pm}_{i})^{d/d_{i}}
sur
{{}^{\xi}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\>q,t}}
, peut être spécialisée à
{q\!=\!\varepsilon}
et fait alors du
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
-module
{{}^{\xi}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\>q,t}}
une représentation de
{U_{\!\!\;t}}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})
dans
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})}
.

6.16
Interpolation de Langlands classique via les groupes quantiques

La théorie des algèbres de Kac-Moody colorées permet de réaliser le principe d’interpolation de Langlands classique à partir des représentations de l’algèbre quantique
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{g})
. Notons que ce résultat suivant est pleinement établi, dans la mesure où la conjecture GQE est vérifiée pour le
I
-coloriage quantique
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}}
, et apporte par ailleurs une nouvelle preuve de la dualité de Langlands entre les catégories
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
et
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})}
(cf. théorème
1
).

On rappelle que
\varepsilon
désigne une racine primitive de l’unité d’ordre
2d
.

Théorème 39
.

Soit
V
une représentation dans
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
et notons par
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
la représentation de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{g})
déformant
V
.

•

L’action de
X^{\pm}_{i}
sur
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
peut être spécialisée à
{q\!=\!1}
et est alors égale à l’action de
X_{i}^{\pm}

sur
V
.

•

L’action de
(X^{\pm}_{i})^{d/d_{i}}
sur
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\;q}}
peut être spécialisée à
{q\!=\!\varepsilon}
et fait alors du
{{}^{L}\!\;\!\mathfrak{h}}
-module
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\;q}}
une représentation de
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
dans
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})}
.

7
Ouvertures

7.1
Dualité de Langlands entre représentations affines

classiques

Soit ici
\mathfrak{g}
une algèbre de Lie simple de dimension finie. On note
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
l’algèbre de Lie affine associée à
\mathfrak{g}
et à un automorphisme
\sigma
d’ordre
m\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
du diagramme de Dynkin de
\mathfrak{g}
: c’est l’extension centrale non triviale de l’algèbre de Lie des applications polynomiales contravariantes de
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
dans
\mathfrak{g}
(on considère les actions de
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
sur
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
et
\mathfrak{g}
, induites respectivement par une racine primitive
dans
\mathbb{C}
d’ordre
m
et par
\sigma
). Lorsque
m=1
, l’algèbre affine
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
est dite non tordue et notée plus simplement
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Lorsque
m>1
, l’algèbre est dite tordue. Remarquons que
\mathfrak{g}
est une sous-algèbre de Lie de l’algèbre de Lie affine non tordue
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
.

L’algèbre de Lie affine
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
n’est pas exactement une algèbre de Kac-Moody, mais plus exactement l’algèbre dérivée d’une algèbre de Kac-Moody
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}}
. La catégorie des représentations de dimension finie de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
est riche et est très différente de la catégorie
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}})
(toute représentation dans
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}})
est par exemple complètement réductible, tandis qu’une représentation de dimension finie de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
ne l’est en général pas).

On note
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}}
l’algèbre Langlands duale de l’algèbre affine
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
: c’est l’algèbre de Lie affine dont l’algèbre de Kac-Moody associée est l’algèbre Langlands duale de l’algèbre de Kac-Moody
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}}
. Dans le cas où l’algèbre
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
est différente de son algèbre Langlands duale, cette dernière est une algèbre de Lie affine tordue si
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
est non tordue et réciproquement.

Notons que
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}}
n’est pas en général une algèbre de Lie affine associée à
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
(les opérations “duale de Langlands” et “affinisation” ne commutent pas). Toutefois, l’algèbre
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}
contient
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
(comme sous-algèbre des applications constantes de l’algèbre de Lie affine
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}
).

Soit maintenant
V
une représentation de dimension finie de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
. On rappelle que la dualité de Langlands, telle que formulée dans le théorème
1
, affirme l’existence d’une extension à
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
de l’action de
{{}^{L}\!\;\!\mathfrak{h}}
sur l’espace
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
(où
V
est regardé comme une représentation de
\mathfrak{g}
par restriction).

Question 40
.

Soit
V
une représentation de dimension finie de l’algèbre de Lie affine non tordue
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Existe-t-il une représentation de
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}
de dimension finie, dont la restriction à la sous-algèbre de Cartan
{{}^{L}\!\;\!\mathfrak{h}}
est la représentation
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
?

Supposons que
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
n’est pas égale à son algèbre de Langlands duale (dans le cas contraire, la question
40
devient triviale). L’algèbre
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}
est une algèbre de Lie affine tordue, on note alors
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
l’algèbre de Lie simple de dimension finie et
\sigma_{1}
l’automorphisme du diagramme de Dynkin de
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
tels que
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}=\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}^{\sigma_{1}}
. En remarquant que l’algèbre
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
est la sous-algèbre de
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
constituée des points fixes de
\sigma_{1}
et en remarquant que toute représentation de dimension finie de
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
peut être étendue en une représentation de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}^{\sigma_{1}}
(par spécialisation à une valeur de
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
des éléments de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}^{\sigma_{1}}
), la question
40
apparaît équivalente à la question suivante : soit
V
une représentation de dimension finie de
\mathfrak{g}
, est-il possible d’étendre à
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
l’action de
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
sur
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
?

Plus généralement, on peut se demander si la dualité de Langlands entre les catégories
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}})
et
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}({{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}}})
admet un analogue pour les représentations de dimension finie de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}}
. Notons pour cela que la correspondance (
4
) entre les sous-algèbres de Cartan de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}}}
peut être restreinte aux sous-algèbres de Cartan
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}}
de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}}
. Notons également que l’action
de
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}
sur une représentation de dimension finie de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
est diagonalisable, on définit alors la sous-représentation
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
de
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}}
de la même manière que précédemment.

Question 41
.

Soit
V
une représentation de dimension finie de l’algèbre de Lie affine
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
. Existe-t-il une représentation de dimension finie de
{{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}}
, dont la restriction à la sous-algèbre de Cartan
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}}
est la représentation
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
.

Rappelons que pour toute représentation
V
dans
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
, il existe une unique représentation dans
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})}
dont la restriction à
{{}^{L}\!\;\!\mathfrak{h}}
est
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V}
. Une propriété analogue d’unicité n’est toutefois pas envisageable dans le cas des questions
40
et
41
. Remarquons enfin qu’une réponse à la question
41
apporte en particulier une réponse à la question
40
, la dualité de Langlands entre les sous-algèbres de Cartan
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{h}}}
étant une extension de la dualité de Langlands entre les sous-algèbres de Cartan de
\mathfrak{g}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}}
.

7.2
Dualité de Langlands entre représentations affines

quantiques

Les algèbres de Lie affines admettent également des déformations quantiques. Soit
\mathfrak{g}
une algèbre de Lie simple de dimension finie, on note
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
l’algèbre affine quantique associée à l’algèbre de Lie affine
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}
. Les questions
40
et
41
, à propos d’une extension de la dualité de Langlands pour les catégories
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}
à une dualité de Langlands entre les représentations de dimensions finies des algèbres de Lie affines, peuvent être alors reformulées dans le cas quantique.

Question 42
.

Soit
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
une représentation de dimension finie de l’algèbre affine quantique non tordue
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
.
Existe-t-il
une représentation de dimension finie
de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
, dont la restriction à
{U_{\!\!\>q}}({{}^{L}\!\;\!\mathfrak{h}})
est la représentation
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\;q}}
?

Question 43
.

Soit
{V_{\!\!\;q}}
une représentation de dimension finie de l’algèbre affine quantique
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
.
Existe-t-il
une représentation de dimension finie de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}({{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}})
, dont la restriction à
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{h}}^{\sigma}}
est la représentation
{{}^{L}\!\!\;V_{\!\!\;q}}
?

La catégorie des représentations de dimension finie de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
, contrairement à la catégorie
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{KM}})
, s’avère être très différente de son analogue classique. Par conséquent, les questions
42
et
43
ne peuvent pas être considérées cette fois-ci équivalentes au problème classique (posé par les questions
40
et
41
).

Frenkel-Hernandez
[
14
]
apportent un début de réponse aux questions
42
et
43
. Ils prouvent en effet l’existence de relations entre certaines représentations de dimension finie des algèbres affines quantiques duales
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
et
{U_{\!\!\>q}}({{}^{L}\!\!\>\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma}})
. Ces relations sont décrites en termes des
q
-caractères desdites représentations (le
q
-caractère
d’une représentation de dimension finie de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
résume une grande partie de la décomposition de Jordan de la représentation sous l’action de la sous-algèbre de Cartan de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sigma})
).

7.3
Correspondance de Langlands géométrique

La dualité de Langlands entre représentations d’algèbres de Kac-Moody peut être reliée au programme de Langlands géométrique. Ce lien a été proposé par Frenkel-Hernandez
[
15
]
, nous reprenons ci-dessous certains des éléments d’explication présentés dans l’introduction
de
[
15
]
, où
\mathfrak{g}
désigne toujours une algèbre de Lie simple de dimension finie.

[
Un des résultats clés dans la correspondance géométrique de Langlands est un isomorphisme entre le centre
Z(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
de l’algèbre enveloppante complétée
de
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
au niveau critique (
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
désigne l’algèbre de Lie affine associée à l’algèbre de dimension
finie
\mathfrak{g}
) et la
{W\!}
-algèbre classique
W({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})
associée à l’algèbre de Lie Langlands duale de
\mathfrak{g}
(voir
[
8
,
9
]
ainsi que
[
18
]
pour plus de détails). Ce résultat constitue le socle de la correspondance de Langlands géométrique locale (
voir
[
10
,
13
]
) et il est aussi un ingrédient principal employé par
Beilinson-Drinfeld
[
1
]
(voir
aussi
[
11
]
) dans la construction de la correspondance de Langlands géométrique globale. Toutefois, cet isomorphisme reste mystérieux : son existence est connue mais mal comprise.

Dans le but de mieux comprendre cet isomorphisme, Frenkel-Reshetikhin
[
16
]
ont proposé de
q
-déformer la situation, en considérant le centre
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
de l’algèbre affine quantique
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
au niveau critique. On sait par ailleurs que le centre
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
est relié à l’anneau de Grothendieck
{\mathrm{Rep}\,{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})}
des représentations de dimension finie de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
(à chaque représentation de dimension finie, il est en effet possible d’associer une série génératrice composée d’éléments dans
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
, en utilisant ce que l’on appelle la construction transfer-matrix). On espère donc obtenir un nouvel éclairage sur l’isomorphisme
Z(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})\simeq W({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})
en étudiant les possibles liens existant entre
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
,
{\mathrm{Rep}\,{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})}
et la
{W\!}
-algèbre classique
q
-déformée

{W_{\!q}}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})
.

L’idée de Frenkel-Reshetikhin
[
17
]
a été de déformer un nouvelle fois la situation, en introduisant une déformation (non commutative) à deux paramètres
{W_{\!q,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
. La spécialisation
{W_{\!q,1}}
à
t=1
est le centre
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
, de telle sorte que
{W_{\!q,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
est une déformation à un paramètre de
Z_{q}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
et une déformation à deux paramètres du centre initial
Z(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
. Le travail de Frenkel-Reshetikhin
[
17
]
a été motivé par l’espoir qu’à travers l’analyse de différentes dualités et limites de
{W_{\!q,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
, il soit possible d’apporter de nouveaux éléments de compréhension à propos de l’isomorphisme
Z(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})\simeq W({{}^{L}\!\!\;\mathfrak{g}})
et par suite à propos de la correspondance de Langlands. En particulier, il a été suggéré que la spécialisation
{W_{\!\varepsilon,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
à
q=\varepsilon
(où
\varepsilon
désigne une racine primitive de l’unité d’ordre
2d
) contient comme sous-algèbre le centre
Z_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
de l’algèbre affine quantique
U_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
au niveau critique. Le centre
Z_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
est par ailleurs lié à l’anneau de Grothendieck
\mathrm{Rep}\,U_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
des représentations de dimension finie de
U_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
(via la construction transfer-matrix). La spécialisation
{W_{\!q,1}}(\mathfrak{g})
à
t=1
est quant à elle liée à l’anneau de Grothendieck des représentations de dimension finie de
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
(toujours via la construction transfer-matrix). La
{W\!}
-algèbre
{W_{\!q,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
apparaît ainsi interpoler les anneaux de Grothendieck des représentations de dimension finie des algèbres affines quantiques associées à
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
et
{{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Ceci suggère en particulier l’existence de certaines relations entre ces représentations (des exemples ont été donnés par Frenkel-Reshetikhin
[
17
]
).
]

On retrouve à travers ces relations la dualité de Langlands entre représentations de dimension finie des algèbres affines quantiques duales
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}})
et
{U_{\!\!\>q}}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
, évoquée précédemment. Précisons toutefois que ces relations sont exprimées au niveau des
q
-caractères et que l’on ignore à ce jour si elles correspondent précisément aux propos de la question
42
.

Remarquons que la valeur de spécialisation
\varepsilon
ait apparut dans trois situations.

•

Une première fois chez Frenkel-Reshetikhin afin de retrouver le centre
Z_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
de l’algèbre affine quantique
U_{t}({{}^{L}\!\!\;\hat{\mathfrak{g}}})
au niveau critique à partir de la
W
-algèbre déformée
{W_{\!q,t}}(\mathfrak{g})
(rappelons qu’il s’agit à ce jour d’une conjecture).

•

Une deuxième fois chez Littelmann et McGerty afin d’établir le
théorème
1
.

•

Une troisième fois dans la conjecture
4
de Frenkel-Hernandez.

partie I
Generalised Quantum Enveloping Algebras

Notations and conventions

Rings and associative algebras are unitary, their morphisms are unity-preserving.

We denote by
\Bbbk
a field of characteristic zero. We denote by
\Bbbk[u]
and by
\Bbbk[u,v]
the polynomial rings
over
\Bbbk
in one variable
u
and in two variables
u,v
, respectively.

Let
\Lambda
be a set. We denote by
\Bbbk\Lambda
the
\Bbbk
-vector space which admits
\Lambda
as a basis. We denote by
\langle\Lambda\rangle
the free monoid generated by
\Lambda
. We denote by
\Bbbk\langle\Lambda\rangle
the
\Bbbk
-algebra
of this monoid. Let
R
be a ring, we denote by
{\Lambda^{\!R}}
the ring of functions from
R
to
\Lambda
.

Let
A
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra and
\mathfrak{g}
be Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra. Left
A
-modules and left
\mathfrak{g}
-modules are called representations of
A
and
\mathfrak{g}
, respectively. Morphisms of left
A
-modules and of left
\mathfrak{g}
-modules are called
A
-morphisms and
\mathfrak{g}
-morphisms, respectively. The universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
is denoted by
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

We identify as usual representations of
\mathfrak{g}
with representations of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

A sum indexed by a empty set is zero, a product indexed by an empty set is equal to
1
.

1
Kac-Moody algebras

We recall in this section the definition of Kac-Moody algebras. Note that the definition given in this paper is slightly different (but mainly equivalent) to the definition of Kac-Moody algebras usually present in the literature. The difference is about the Cartan subalgebra, which is built here from what we call a root datum (we borrow the name and the concept from Lusztig: see
[
35
]
). We also recall some results on the representation theory of Kac-Moody algebras.

1.1
Generalised Cartan matrices and root data

A
generalised Cartan matrix
is a matrix
C={(C_{ij})}_{i,j\in I}
indexed by a finite
set
I
, with entries in
\mathbb{Z}
and which satisfies the following conditions for all
i,j\in I
.

•

C_{ii}=2
.

•

C_{ij}\leq 0
if
i\neq j
.

•

C_{ij}=0
if and only if
C_{ji}=0
.

The set
I
is called the
Dynkin set
. The elements of
I
are called the
Dynkin vertices
. The generalised Cartan matrix
C
is called
symmetrisable
if there exists a family
{(d_{i})}_{i\in I}
, called a
symmetrising vector
of
C
, with values in
\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and such that
d_{i}\>\!C_{ij}=d_{j}\>\!C_{ji}
for
all
i,j

in
I
. We assume that the integers
d_{i}
(
i\in I^{\prime}
) are coprime for every connected component
I^{\prime}
of
I
(two elements
i,j\in I
are linked if
C_{ij}\neq 0
). In this paper,
we always assume that a generalised Cartan matrix is symmetrisable
.

A generalised Cartan matrix is of
finite type
if it is positive-definite. Generalised Cartan matrices of finite type are also called Cartan matrices.

A
root datum

\mathfrak{X}
associated to a generalised Cartan matrix
C
consists of the following.

•

A pair
(X,Y)
where
X
,
Y
are two finitely generated free
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

•

Families
(\alpha_{i}\>\!;\,i\in I)
and
{({\check{\alpha}_{i}}\>\!;\,i\in I)}
with values in
X
and
Y
, respectively. We demand that the maps
i\mapsto\alpha_{i}
and
i\mapsto\check{\alpha}_{i}
are injective.

•

A perfect bilinear pairing
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle:Y\times X\to\mathbb{Z}
such that
\langle{\check{\alpha}_{i}},\alpha_{j}\rangle=C_{ij}
for all
i,j\in I
.

The
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
X
and
Y
are called the
root lattice
and the
coroot lattice
, respectively. We call
\alpha_{i}
and
{\check{\alpha}_{i}}
(
i\in I
) the
simple roots
and the
simple coroots
, respectively. Where there is no possibility for confusion, we normally abuse notation slightly and write
(X,Y,I)
to designate the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
. The root datum is said of
finite type
if the generalised Cartan matrix
C
is of finite type.

We say that the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is
X\!
-regular
if the family of simple roots is linearly independent and
Y\!
-regular
if the family of simple coroots is. When
\mathfrak{X}
is of finite type,
X\!
-regularity and
Y\!
-regularity are automatic by the non-degeneracy of
C
, but in general it is an additional assumption, which we make in this paper.

An element
\lambda
in
X
is said
dominant
if
\langle{\check{\alpha}_{i}},\lambda\rangle\geq 0
for all
i\in I
. The subset of dominant elements in
X
is denoted by
{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
and called the
dominant cone
.

On the root lattice
X
, we define a partial order (remark that the antisymmetry axiom is satisfied thanks to the
X\!
-regularity):

\lambda\ \leq\ \lambda^{\prime}\quad\ \text{ if }\ \quad\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda\ \in\ \sum_{i\in I}\,\mathbb{N}\>\!\alpha_{i}\,.

1.2
Kac-Moody algebras with and without Serre relations

The
Kac-Moody algebra

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}(\mathfrak{X})
associated to the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
and subject to the following relations:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rclr}\big[\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\big]&=&0&(\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[{\check{\mu}},X^{\pm}_{i}\big]&=&\pm\;\!\langle{\check{\mu}},\alpha_{i}\rangle\,X_{i}^{\pm}&(i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{j}\big]&=&0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,,\end{array}}\right.

(10)

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lr}\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]=\check{\alpha}_{i}&(i\in I)\,,\\
\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose k}\,{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}\;\!X^{\pm}_{j}\;\!{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}=0&\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

(11)

The relations
\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}{1-C_{ij}\choose k}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}X^{\pm}_{j}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}=0
(
i,j\in I
,
i\neq j
) are called the
Serre relations
. The elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
) are called the
Chevalley generators
of
\mathfrak{g}
.

We denote by
{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U^{>0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the
\Bbbk
-subalgebras of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i}
(
i\in I
) and
X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), respectively. We denote by
{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the
\Bbbk
-subalgebra
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
generated by
Y
. The algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
, when viewed as a
\Bbbk
-vector space, admits the following decomposition, where the isomorphism is given by the multiplication of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
:

{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\ \simeq\ \,{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\otimes_{\Bbbk}\,{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\otimes_{\Bbbk}\,{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,.

We call this decomposition the
triangular decomposition
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

The Kac-Moody algebra without Serre relations

\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathfrak{X})
associated to the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the relations (
10
) and to the following ones:

\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]=\check{\alpha}_{i}\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

(12)

We denote by
\varpi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})
the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. The algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
also admits a triangular decomposition.

1.3
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
and the simple
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-directions

We designate by
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
the root datum defined by the following.

•

The root and coroot lattices are both equal to
\mathbb{Z}
.

•

The simple root and the simple coroot are
2\in\mathbb{Z}
and
1\in\mathbb{Z}
, respectively.

•

The pairing is the product of
\mathbb{Z}
.

Note that the Kac-Moody algebra associated to
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
is naturally isomorphic to the Lie algebra of 2-by-2 matrices with coefficients in
\Bbbk
and whose trace is zero; we denote it by
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

The two Chevalley generators of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
are designated by
X^{-}
and
X^{+}
, we besides denote by
H
the simple coroot of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
. The Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is generated by the elements
X^{-},X^{+}
,
H
and subject to the following relations:

\displaystyle\big[H,X^{\pm}\big]

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle\pm\;\!2\,X^{\pm}\,,

(13)

\displaystyle\big[X^{+},X^{-}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H\,.

(14)

Remark that there is no Serre relations for
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

Let
i\in I
. There exist unique Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra morphisms from
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
to
\mathfrak{g}

and
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}
, which
sends
X^{\pm}
and
H
to
X_{i}^{\pm}
and
\check{\alpha}_{i}
, respectively. The induced
\Bbbk
-algebra morphisms from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
are denoted by
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
, respectively. The morphisms
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
(
i\in I
) are called the
simple
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-directions

of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
, respectively.

1.4
Highest weight representations and the category
\mathcal{O}

Let
V
be a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
and let
\lambda\in X
. A vector
v
in
V
is called a
weight vector
of weight
\lambda
if
\check{\mu}.v=\langle\check{\mu},\lambda\rangle\;\!v
for all
\check{\mu}\in Y
. The
weight space

of
V
of
weight
\lambda
is the
\Bbbk
-vector subspace of
V
whose vectors are of weight
\lambda
, we denote it
by
V_{\lambda}
. Remark that the sum of the weight spaces of
V
is direct. The representation
V
is called a
weight representation

of
\mathfrak{g}

if
V
is equal to the sum of its weights spaces. If
V
is a weight representation and if
V_{\lambda}
is nonzero, we call
\lambda
a
weight
of
V
. The set of weights of a weight
representation
V
is denoted
by
\mathrm{wt}(V)
.

A representation
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
is called a
highest weight representation
of
\mathfrak{g}
of highest weight
\lambda
if the following two conditions hold.

•

The representation
V
is generated by a nonzero vector
v_{\lambda}
in
V_{\lambda}
. The vector
v_{\lambda}
is called a
highest weight vector
of
V
.

•

The weights of
V
are less than or equal to
\lambda
.

Remark that a highest weight representation of
\mathfrak{g}
is a weight representation
of
\mathfrak{g}
with finite-dimensional weight spaces.

We denote by
{\mathcal{O}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the category of representations
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
which satisfy the following conditions.

•

The representation
V
is a weight representation of
\mathfrak{g}
with finite-dimensional weight spaces.

•

There exist finitely many elements
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k}
in
X
such that
\mathrm{wt}(V)
is contained in
\bigcup_{s=1}^{k}(\lambda_{s}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}\alpha_{i})
.

1.5
Some known results

A weight representation
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
is called
integrable
if the actions of the Chevalley generators on
V
are locally nilpotent. We denote by
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the category of integrable representations of
\mathfrak{g}
. When the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is of finite type (equivalently, when
\mathfrak{g}
is finite-dimensional), a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
is in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
if and only if it is finite-dimensional.

Let us recall some results on the representation theory of
\mathfrak{g}
.

•

For every
\lambda\in X
, there is a unique, up to isomorphism, irreducible representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of
\mathfrak{g}
of highest weight
\lambda
.

•

Let
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
. The representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
is generated by a vector
v_{\lambda}
and subject to the following relations:

\check{\mu}.v_{\lambda}\,=\,\langle\lambda,\check{\mu}\rangle\;\!v_{\lambda}\,,\quad X^{+}_{i}.v_{\lambda}=0\,,\quad(X^{-}_{i})^{\langle\lambda,\check{\alpha}_{i}\rangle+1}.v_{\lambda}\,=\,0\quad\ (i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,.

•

The representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
(
\lambda\in X
) are pairwise distinct and are the unique, up to isomorphism, irreducible representations in
{\mathcal{O}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

•

Let
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in X
. Every
\mathfrak{g}
-morphism from
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
to
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda^{\prime})}
is equal to a scalar multiple of the identity map if
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
and is zero else.

•

Let
\lambda\in X
. The representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
is integrable if and only if
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
.

•

Every representation in the category
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
with
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
.

We give below, for the reader’s convenience, a proof of a complete-reducibility type result for the category of integrable representations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

Proposition 1.1
.

An integrable representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Besides, a representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is integrable if and only if the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
are locally finite.

Proof.

Let
V
be a representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
on which the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
are locally finite and let
v
be a vector in
V
. Denote by
V^{\prime}
the subrepresentation of
V
generated by
v
. In view of the triangular decomposition of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
and since the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
on
V
are locally finite, the subrepresentation
V^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional. Therefore,
V^{\prime}
is a sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. This being true for
every
v
in
V
, the representation
V
is a sum of irreducible representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the representation
V
is a direct sum of irreducible representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

The author ignores whether there exists (when
\mathfrak{g}
is not finite-dimensional) a reference for the following proposition. The proof given below follows from a private communication with Victor Kac. Note that it is possible in the case
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
to give a more direct and elementary proof.

Proposition 1.2
.

An element in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the representations of
\mathfrak{g}
in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proof.

Consider the
{X\!}
-gradation of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
defined by the following:

\mathrm{deg}\;\!(\mu)\,:=\,0\,,\quad\quad\mathrm{deg}\;\!(X^{\pm}_{i})\,:=\,\alpha_{i}\quad\quad\quad(\mu\in X,\ i\in I)\,.

Let
x
be a nonzero element in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. Recall the triangular decomposition of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and pick nonzero homogeneous
elements
x^{-}_{k}
,
x^{0}_{k}

and
x^{+}_{k}

in
{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
,
{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U^{>0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
respectively
(
1\leq k\leq l
)
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

a)

x=\sum_{1\leq k\leq l}x^{-}_{k}\;\!x^{0}_{k}\;\!x_{k}^{+}
,

b)

the family
{(x^{-}_{k})}_{1\leq k\leq l}
is
\Bbbk
-linearly independent,

c)

\deg(x_{1}^{+})\ngtr\deg(x_{k}^{+})
for every
1<k\leq l
.

Denote by
\tau
the involutive anti-automorphism of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
which sends
X_{i}^{\pm}

to
-X_{i}^{\mp}
for every
i\in I
and which fixes pointwise
Y
. Pick
\lambda
in
{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
such that the following conditions hold (we denote by
v_{\lambda}
a fixed choice of highest weight vector of the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
):

a’)

x^{0}_{1}(\lambda)
is nonzero,

b’)

the family
{(x^{-}_{k}.v_{\lambda})}_{1\leq k\leq l}
is
\Bbbk
-linearly independent,

c’)

the vector
\tau(x^{+}_{1}).v_{\lambda}
is nonzero.

Pick now a nonzero
\Bbbk
-bilinear form
\sigma
on
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
which has the following properties:

a”)

\sigma
is non-degenerate,

b”)

\sigma(v,w)=0
for every weight vectors
v,w
in
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of distinct weights,

c”)

\sigma
is contravariant, i.e.
\sigma(y.v,w)=\sigma(v,\tau(y).w)
for every
v,w\in{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
and for every
y\in{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

In view of the non-degeneracy of
\sigma
and in view of the orthogonality property
b^{\prime\prime})
, pick a weight vector
v
in
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of weight
\lambda-\deg(x_{1}^{+})
such that
\sigma(v,\tau(x^{+}_{1}).v_{\lambda})
is nonzero. Since
\sigma
is contravariant, the vector
x_{1}^{+}.v
is thus a nonzero scalar multiple of
v_{\lambda}
. Thanks to condition
c)
above, the following holds:

x.v\ =\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq l,\\
\deg(x^{+}_{k})\,=\,\deg(x_{1}^{+})\end{subarray}}x^{0}_{k}(\lambda)\,x^{-}_{k}.(x^{+}_{k}.v)\,.

In view of conditions
a^{\prime})
and
b^{\prime})
, one concludes that
x.v
is nonzero.
∎

2
Modules and algebras over a power series ring

2.1
The power series ring
\Bbbk[|h|]

We denote by
\Bbbk[|h|]
the
power series ring
in the variable
h
over
\Bbbk
. We use the following notation to describe an element
\lambda_{h}
in
\Bbbk[|h|]
:

\lambda_{h}\ =\ \sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\,\lambda_{m}\,h^{m}\,;\quad\text{ with }\ \lambda_{m}\in\Bbbk\,,\ \ \forall\,m\in\mathbb{N}\,.

The formal power series
\lambda_{h}
is invertible in
\Bbbk[|h|]
if and only if the scalar
\lambda_{0}
is nonzero.

2.2
h
-adic topology

Let
V
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module. The submodules
{h^{m}\>\!V}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) form a local basis at the origin of a linear topology, called the
h
-adic topology
on the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
.
In this first part of the thesis, topological statements are always made with regards to the
h
-adic topology.
We recall below some definitions and facts about the
h
-adic topology.

A
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear map is automatically continuous. In particular, the action by every element in the ring
\Bbbk[|h|]
on a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module is continuous. The closure in a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module of a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule is again a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule.

Let
A
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra, let
\Lambda
be a set and let
x_{l},y_{l}
(
l\in\Lambda
) be elements
in
A
. The representation of
A

topologically generated
by the vector
v
and subject to the set of relations
{\{x_{l}.v=y_{l}.v\;\!;\,l\in\Lambda\}}
is the quotient of the left regular representation of
A
by the closure of the subrepresentation generated by the vectors
x_{l}-y_{l}
(
l\in\Lambda
).

The space
V
is
separated
if and only if
\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{h^{m}\>\!V}=(0)
, i.e. if and only if the submodule
(0)
is closed in
V
. As a consequence, a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule
W
is closed in
V
if and only if the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V/W
is separated.

A sequence
{(v_{l})}_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
V
is said to be
of Cauchy
if it satisfies the following condition:

\forall\,m\in\mathbb{N}\,,\ \exists\,l(m)\in\mathbb{N}\,,\ \forall\,l\geq l(m)\,,\quad(v_{l+1}\,-\,v_{l})\ \in\ h^{m}\;\!V\,.

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
is
complete
if all the Cauchy sequences with values in
V
are convergent. Note that a quotient of a complete
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module is also complete. The
completion
of
V
is the unique (up to a unique isomorphism) complete and separated
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module which contains
V
as a dense
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule.

The
topologically direct sum
of a family
{(V_{l})}_{l\in\Lambda}
of
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules is the completion of the usual direct sum
\bigoplus_{l\in\Lambda}V_{l}
.

2.3
Topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules

Let
V_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-vector space. We denote by
V_{0}[|h|]
the
\Bbbk
-vector space of functions from
\mathbb{N}
to
V_{0}
. We use the following notation to describe an element
v_{h}
in
V_{0}[|h|]
:

v_{h}\ =\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\,v_{m}\,h^{m}\,,\quad\text{ where }\ v_{m}\ :=\ v_{h}(m)\quad(m\in\mathbb{N})\,.

We endow
V_{0}[|h|]
with a structure of
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module:

\lambda_{h}\,v_{h}\ :=\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\ \sum_{p+q=m}\,\lambda_{p}\,v_{q}\,\right)h^{m}\quad\ \ \big(\lambda_{h}\in\Bbbk[|h|]\,,\ v_{h}\in V_{0}[|h|]\big)\,.

We identify
V_{0}
in a canonical way with a
\Bbbk
-submodule of
V_{0}[|h|]
.

A
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
is said
topologically free
if there exists a
\Bbbk
-vector space
V_{0}
and a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear isomorphism
f
from
V_{0}[|h|]
to
V
. The image by
f
of a basis of the
\Bbbk
-vector space
V_{0}
is called a
basis
of topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
. A topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module is torsion-free, separated and complete. The converse is also true: see for example
[
28
, proposition XVI.2.4]
.

Let
f_{0}:V_{0}\to V_{0}^{\prime}
be a
\Bbbk
-linear map. We denote by
f_{0}[|h|]
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear map from
V_{0}[|h|]
to
V^{\prime}_{0}[|h|]
which makes the following diagram commute.

\textstyle{V_{0}[|h|]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}[|h|]}
\textstyle{V_{0}^{\prime}[|h|]}
\textstyle{V_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{\,V^{\prime}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Let
A_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra. We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
A_{0}[|h|]
with a structure of
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra:

x_{h}\,x^{\prime}_{h}\ :=\ \sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\ \sum_{p+q=n}\,x_{p}\,x_{q}\,\right)h^{m}\quad\ \ \big(x_{h},x^{\prime}_{h}\in A_{0}[|h|]\big)\,.

Remark that
A
is a
\Bbbk
-subalgebra of
A[|h|]
. Remark also that if
f:A\to A^{\prime}
is a
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism, then
f[|h|]:A[|h|]\to A^{\prime}[|h|]
is a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism.

Let
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda
be two sets and let
x_{l},y_{l}
(
l\in\Lambda
) be elements in the algebra
(\Bbbk\Lambda_{0})[|h|]
. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
topologically generated
by the set
\Lambda_{0}
and subject to the set of relations
{\{x_{l}=y_{l}\;\!;\,l\in\Lambda\}}
is the quotient of the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
(\Bbbk\Lambda_{0})[|h|]
by the closure of the two-sided ideal generated by the elements
y_{l}-x_{l}
(
l\in\Lambda
).

Let
V_{0}
be a representation of
A_{0}
. We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V_{0}[|h|]
with a structure of representation of
A_{0}[|h|]
:

x_{h}\!\>.\!\>v_{h}\ :=\ \sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\ \sum_{p+q=n}\,x_{p}\!\>.\!\>v_{q}\,\right)h^{m}\quad\ \ \big(x_{h}\in A_{0}[|h|],\ v_{h}\in V_{0}[|h|]\big)\,.

2.4
Specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}

Let
V
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module. The
\Bbbk
-vector space
V/hV
is denoted by
V_{|h=0}
and we call it the
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
V
. We denote by
\pi_{|h=0}^{V}
the projection map from
V
to
V_{|h=0}
. Let
V_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-vector space, we identify in a canonical way the
\Bbbk
-vectors spaces
V_{0}[|h|]_{|h=0}
and
V_{0}
.

Let
{f\!:\!V\!\to\!V^{\prime}}
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear map. The
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
f
is the
\Bbbk
-linear map
f_{|h=0}
from
V_{|h=0}
to
V^{\prime}_{|h=0}
induced by
f
. Here are some facts.

•

Assume that
V
is complete and that
V^{\prime}
is separated. The map
f
is surjective if and only if
f_{|h=0}
is surjective.

•

Assume that
V
is separated and that
V^{\prime}
is torsion-free. If the map
f_{|h=0}
is injective, then
f
is injective. The converse statement is false in general (consider for example the action of
h
on
V^{\prime}
).

•

Assume that
V
is topologically free. A family
{(v_{l})}_{l\in\Lambda}
with values
in
V
is a basis of the topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
if and only if
(\pi_{|h=0}^{V}{(v_{l}))}_{l\in\Lambda}
is a basis of the
\Bbbk
-vector space
V_{|h=0}
.

Let
A
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra. We regard
A_{|h=0}
as a
\Bbbk
-algebra and we call it the
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
A
. The projection
map
\pi_{|h=0}^{A}
from
A
to
A_{|h=0}
is then a
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism. Let
A_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra, remark that the
\Bbbk
-algebras
A_{0}[|h|]_{|h=0}
and
A_{0}
are equal. Let
g:A\to A^{\prime}
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism, remark that the map
g_{|h=0}
is a
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism. Let
V
be a representation of
A
. We regard
V_{|h=0}
as a representation of
A_{|h=0}
and we call it the
the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the representation
V
. Here are some conventions.

•

The specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
topologically generated
by a set
\Lambda_{0}
and subject to a set of relations
{\{x_{l}=y_{l}\;\!;\,l\in\Lambda\}}
is identified with the quotient of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
\Bbbk\Lambda_{0}
by the two-sided ideal generated by the elements
\pi_{|h=0}^{(\Bbbk\Lambda_{0})[|h|]}(y_{l}-x_{l})
(
l\in\Lambda^{\prime}
).

•

The specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the representation of
A

topologically generated
by the vector
v
and subject to a set of relations
{\{x_{l}.v=y_{l}.v\;\!;\,l\in\Lambda\}}
is identified with the quotient of the left regular representation of
A_{|h=0}
by the subrepresentation generated by the vectors
\pi_{|h=0}^{A}(y_{l}-x_{l})
(
l\in\Lambda
).

3
Pointed diagrams of algebras

and formal deformations

Let
R
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra. We denote by
{\mathscr{A}_{\!\!\>R}}
the category of
R
-algebras and by
{\mathbb{F}_{\!R}}
the forgetful functor
from
{\mathscr{A}_{\!\!\>R}}
to
{\mathscr{A}_{\Bbbk}}
.

We denote
by
\circ
the horizontal composition for natural transformations and
by
\ast
the vertical composition. We denote also by
\circ
the composition for functors.

We denote by
\!\mathrm{\it{Cat}}
the category of categories (the morphisms are the functors). We denote by
\mathrm{\it{Fun}}(\mathscr{C}^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime})
the category of functors from a category
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
to another category
\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}
(the morphisms are the natural transformations).

3.1
Pointed diagrams of algebras

Let
\mathscr{S}
be a category. A
R
-diagram
(of algebras) of shape
\mathscr{S}
is a functor
from
\mathscr{S}
to
{\mathscr{A}_{\!\!\>R}}
. When there is no risk of confusion, we do not specify the shape of a diagram.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a
\Bbbk
-diagram. We say that a
R
-diagram
\mathbb{A}
is
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
if it is endowed with a natural
transformation
\dot{\mathbb{A}}
from
\mathbb{D}

to
{\mathbb{F}_{\!R}}\circ\mathbb{A}
. We call
\dot{\mathbb{A}}
the
\mathbb{D}
-point

of
\mathbb{A}
.

The identity map on a
\Bbbk
-diagram
\mathbb{D}
endows
\mathbb{D}
with a structure of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram. We always refer to this canonical structure, when dealing with
\mathbb{D}
as a
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram.

Let
\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
be two
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
R
-diagrams. A
morphism of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
R
-diagram
from
\mathbb{A}
to
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
is a natural transformation
\alpha
from
\mathbb{A}
to
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
such that
({\mathbb{F}_{\!R}}\circ\alpha)\ast\dot{\mathbb{A}}
and
\dot{\mathbb{A}}^{\prime}
are equal.

We denote by
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathbb{D})}
the category of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
R
-diagrams. The morphisms of
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathbb{D})}
are the morphisms of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
R
-diagrams, their composition is the natural one.

Remark that
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathbb{D})}
is a comma category. Remark also that
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\bullet)}
defines a contravariant functor from
\mathrm{\it{Fun}}(\mathscr{S},{\mathscr{A}_{\Bbbk}})
to
\!\mathrm{\it{Cat}}
. Let
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
be another
\Bbbk
-diagram and let
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
be a natural transformation from
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
to
\mathbb{D}
. The following holds.

1)

The (covariant) functor
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}})}
is faithful,

2)

If
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
is an epimorphism in
\mathrm{\it{Fun}}(\mathscr{S},{\mathscr{A}_{\Bbbk}})
, then the functor
{\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{\!\!\>R}(\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}})}
is full.

3.2
Formal deformation of pointed diagrams

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram. The
\Bbbk
-diagram
\bullet_{|h=0}\circ\mathbb{A}
is denoted by
\mathbb{A}_{|h=0}
and called the
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram

\mathbb{A}
.

Denote by
\varpi_{|h=0}
the natural transformation from
{\mathbb{F}_{\!\!\>\Bbbk[|h|]}}
to
\bullet_{|h=0}
defined by the following: let
A
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra, the
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism
{{(\varpi_{|h=0})}_{\!A}}
is the projection map
\pi_{|h=0}^{A}
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a
\Bbbk
-diagram and let
\dot{\mathbb{A}}
be a
\mathbb{D}
-point of
\mathbb{A}
. We
endow
\mathbb{A}_{|h=0}
with the
\mathbb{D}
-point

(\varpi_{|h=0}\circ\mathbb{A})\ast\dot{\mathbb{A}}
and we call the
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram

\mathbb{A}_{|h=0}
the
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram
\mathbb{A}
.

Let
\alpha:\mathbb{A}\Rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
be a natural transformation between two
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagrams
. We denote by
\alpha_{|h=0}
the natural transformation
\bullet_{|h=0}\circ\alpha
from
\mathbb{A}_{|h=0}
to
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{|h=0}
and we call it the
specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the natural transformation
\alpha
. Remark that the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of a morphism of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram is a morphism of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\mathbb{A}_{0}
be a
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram of shape
\mathscr{S}
. A formal deformation of the
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram

\mathbb{A}_{0}
is a
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram
\mathbb{A}_{h}
which satisfy the following two conditions.

•

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
\mathbb{A}_{h}(s)
is topologically free for every object
s
of
\mathscr{S}
.

•

The
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagrams
(\mathbb{A}_{h})_{|h=0}
and
\mathbb{A}_{0}
are isomorphic.

3.3
Trivial formal deformations

Denote by
\iota
the natural transformation from the identity functor on
{\mathscr{A}_{\Bbbk}}
to the functor
{\mathbb{F}_{\!\!\>\Bbbk[|h|]}}\circ({\bullet\>\![|h|]})
, defined by the following: let
A
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra, the
\Bbbk
-algebra

morphism
{\iota_{\!A}}
is the inclusion map from
A
to
A[|h|]
.

The
constant formal deformation

\mathbb{A}_{0}[|h|]
of a
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram
\mathbb{A}_{0}
is the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram
({\bullet\>\![|h|]})\circ\mathbb{A}_{0}
, endowed with the
\mathbb{D}
-point
(\iota\circ\mathbb{A}_{0})\ast\dot{\mathbb{A}}_{0}
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
\mathbb{D},\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
be two
\Bbbk
-diagrams of shape
\mathscr{S}
, let
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
be a natural transformation
from
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
to
\mathbb{D}
and let
\mathbb{A}_{h}
be a formal deformation of a
\mathbb{D}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram
\mathbb{A}_{0}
.

1)

An isomorphism
\alpha_{h}
of
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram

from
({\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{h}(\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}})})(\mathbb{A}_{0}[|h|])

to
({\dot{\mathscr{A}}_{h}(\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}})})(\mathbb{A}_{h})
whose specialisation
at
{h\!=\!0}
is a morphism of
\mathbb{D}
-pointed

\Bbbk
-diagram
is called a
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
-trivialisation of the formal deformation
\mathbb{A}_{h}
.

2)

The formal deformation
\mathbb{A}_{h}
is said
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
-trivial if it admits a
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
-trivialisation.

3)

The formal deformation
\mathbb{A}_{h}
is said locally
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}
-trivial if for every object
s
of
\mathscr{S}
, the formal deformation
\mathbb{A}_{h}(s)
of the
\mathbb{D}(s)
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
\mathbb{A}_{0}(s)
is
\frac{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{D}}(s)
-trivial.

3.4
Formal deformation of representations

Denote by
\mathbf{1}
a category with one element and one morphism. We identify in a canonical way the category
\mathrm{\it{Fun}}(\mathbf{1},{\mathscr{A}_{\!\!\>R}})
with the category
{\mathscr{A}_{\!\!\>R}}
: a
R
-diagram of shape
\mathbf{1}
is a
R
-algebra. Thanks to this identification, one can view
R
-algebras as a particular case of
R
-diagrams. In particular, the previous subsections yield the notions of
D
-pointed
R
-algebras
, where
D
is a
\Bbbk
-algebra, and of
formal deformations of pointed algebras
. We use the following notational convention: an element
x
in
D
can be employed to designate the element
\dot{A}(x)
in
A
.

Let
A_{0}
be a
D
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra, let
A_{h}
be a formal deformation of
A_{0}
and
let
V_{0}
be a representation of
A_{0}
. We assume that the
D
-point
\dot{A}_{0}
of
A_{0}
is surjective and we denote by
f_{0}
the unique morphism of
D
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra from
A_{0}
to the specialisation of
A_{h}
at
{h\!=\!0}
.

Definition 3.4
.

A formal deformation of
V_{0}
along
A_{h}
is a representation
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
of
A_{h}
which satisfies the following two conditions.

•

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
is topologically free.

•

The pull-back of
(V_{h})_{|h=0}
by
f_{0}
is isomorphic to
V_{0}
.

Two formal deformations along
A_{h}
of
V_{0}
are said
isomorphic
if they are isomorphic representations of
A_{h}
.

3.5
Relative Hochschild cohomology

Let
A_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra, let
B_{0}
be a
\Bbbk
-subalgebra of
A_{0}
, let
M
be a
A_{0}
-bimodule and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote by
C^{n}(A_{0},M)
the
\Bbbk
-vector space formed by the
\Bbbk
-multilinear
maps from
(A_{0})^{n}
to
M
and we denote by
C^{n}(A_{0},B_{0},M)
the
\Bbbk
-vector
subspace of
C^{n}(A_{0},M)
formed by the maps
f
for which the following condition holds:

f(y_{1}\!\>x_{1},y_{2}\!\>x_{2},\dots,y_{n}\!\>x_{n}).\!\>y_{n+1}\ =\ y_{1}\!\>.f(x_{1}\!\>y_{2},\dots,x_{n-1}\!\>y_{n},x_{n}\!\>y_{n+1})\,,\\
\forall\,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in A_{0}\,,\ \forall\,y_{1},\dots,y_{n+1}\in B_{0}\,.

We denote by
d_{n}
the
\Bbbk
-linear map from
C^{n}(A_{0},M)
to
C^{n+1}(A_{0},M)
defined by the following for
f\in C^{n}(A_{0},M)
and for
x_{1},\dots,x_{n+1}\in A_{0}
:

\displaystyle\big[d_{n}(f)\big]\!\>(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n+1})

\displaystyle\ :=

x_1 ​   . f(x_2, ⋯, x_n+1)

\displaystyle+\

∑_l=1^n-1 f(x_1, …, x_l-1, x_l ​   x_l+1, x_l+2, ⋯, x_n)

\displaystyle+\

f(x_1, ⋯, x_n) . ​   x_n+1   .

The
\Bbbk
-vector spaces
C^{n}(A_{0},M)
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) and the maps
d_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) form a complex
C^{\bullet}(A_{0},M)
. The
\Bbbk
-vector subspaces
C^{n}(A_{0},B_{0},M)
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) form a subcomplex
C^{\bullet}(A_{0},B_{0},M)
. The cohomology
H^{\bullet}(A_{0},B_{0},M)
of
C^{\bullet}(A_{0},B_{0},M)
is called the
relative Hochschild cohomology
of the pair
(A_{0},B_{0})
with coefficients in
M
.

Let
\mu_{h}
be a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra product on the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
A_{0}[|h|]
. We denote then
by
A_{0}[|\mu_{h}|]
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra whose underlying
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
is
A_{0}[|h|]
and whose product is equal to
\mu_{h}
. We denote
by
\mu_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) the maps in
C^{2}(A_{0},A_{0})
such that the following holds:

\mu_{h}(x_{1},x_{2})\ =\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{m}(x_{1},x_{2})\!\;h^{m}\,,\quad\quad\ \forall\,x_{1},x_{2}\in A_{0}\,.

We assume that the product
\mu_{h}
satisfies the following two conditions.

•

The map
\mu_{0}
is equal to the product of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
A_{0}
.

•

The map
\mu_{m}
belongs to
C^{2}(A_{0},B_{0},A_{0})
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let now
V_{0}
be a representation of
A_{0}
and let
{V_{\!\!\;h}},{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\>h}}
be two representations
of
A_{0}[|\mu_{h}|]
whose underlying
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules are both equal to
V_{0}[|h|]
. We denote by
\pi_{h}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{h}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphisms from
A_{0}[|\mu_{h}|]
to
{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk[|h|]}}{(V_{0}[|h|])}
associated to the representations
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
and
{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\>h}}
, respectively. We denote
by
\pi_{m}

and
\pi^{\prime}_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) the maps in
C^{2}(A_{0},{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk}}{(V_{0})})
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\big[\pi_{h}(x)\big](v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\big[\pi_{m}(x)\big]\!\!\;(v)\!\;h^{m}\,,\quad\quad\ \forall\,x\in A_{0}\,,\ \forall\,v\in V_{0}\,,

\displaystyle\big[\pi^{\prime}_{h}(x)\big](v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m}(x)\big]\!\!\;(v)\!\;h^{m}\,,\quad\quad\ \forall\,x\in A_{0}\,,\ \forall\,v\in V_{0}\,.

We assume that the representations
{V_{\!\!\;h}},{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\>h}}
satisfy the following two conditions.

•

The maps
\pi_{0}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{0}
are both equal to the
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism from
A_{0}
to
{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk}}{(V_{0})}
associated to the representation
V_{0}
.

•

The maps
\pi_{m}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{m}
belong to
C^{2}(A_{0},B_{0},{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk}}{(V_{0})})
for
every
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

An extension of two representations of
A_{0}
is said
B_{0}
-trivial
if the restriction to
B_{0}
is a trivial extension in the category of representations of
B_{0}
.

Proposition 3.5
.

If every
B_{0}
-trivial extension of
V_{0}
by
V_{0}
is
A_{0}
-trivial, then there exists a
A_{0}[|\mu_{h}|]
-isomorphism between
V_{h}
and
V^{\prime}_{h}
whose specialisation
at
{h\!=\!0}
is the identity map on
V_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that every
B_{0}
-trivial extension of the representation
V_{0}
by itself is
A_{0}
-trivial; i.e. suppose that the cohomology group
H^{1}(A_{0},B_{0},{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk}}{(V_{0}}))
is zero. Let
f_{0}
be the identity map on
V_{0}
and let us construct by induction
B_{0}
-endomorphisms
{f_{\!\!\;m}}
of
V_{0}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) such that the following assertion holds for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m}\,{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x)\big]\ =\ \sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m}\,\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x)\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\,,\quad\ \forall\,x\in A_{0}\,.

(
\ast_{m}
)

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that we have constructed
B_{0}
-endomorphisms
{f_{\!\!\;m^{\prime}}}
of
V_{0}
such that assertion
(\ast_{m^{\prime}})
holds for
every
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{<m}
. Since
H^{1}(A_{0},B_{0},{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk}}{(V_{0}}))
is zero, since the maps
{f_{\!\!\;m^{\prime}}}
are
B_{0}
-morphisms for all
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<m}
by induction hypothesis and in view of the conditions satisfied by
\pi_{h},\pi^{\prime}_{h}
, there exists a
B_{0}
-endomorphism
{f_{\!\!\;m}}
of
V_{0}
such that assertion
(\ast_{m})
holds if and only if the following equality holds for
every
x_{1},x_{2}\in A_{0}
:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg(\big[\pi_{0}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{0}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
+\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{0}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{0}(x_{2})\big]\bigg)\\
=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1}\!\>x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{1}\!\>x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\,.

Besides, since
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
and
{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\>h}}
are representations of
A_{0}[|\mu_{h}|]
, the following equalities hold for every
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and for every
x_{1},x_{2}\in A_{0}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m^{\prime}}\pi_{m_{1}}\big(\mu_{m_{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m^{\prime}}\big[\pi_{m_{1}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m^{\prime}}\pi^{\prime}_{m_{1}}\big(\mu_{m_{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m^{\prime}}\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{1}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\,.

As a consequence, the following equality holds for every
x_{1},x_{2}\in A_{0}
:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1}\!\>x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{1}\!\>x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
m_{2}+m_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{m_{3}}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{3}}(x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
-\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
m_{3}\neq m\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}\big(\mu_{m_{3}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\big(\mu_{m_{3}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\,.

Since by induction hypothesis assertion
(\ast_{m^{\prime}})
holds for every
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<m}
, the following equalities then do for every
x_{1},x_{2}\in A_{0}
:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
m_{3}\neq m\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}\big(\mu_{m_{3}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\big(\mu_{m_{3}}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\ =\ 0\,,\\
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
m_{1}\neq m\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{m_{3}}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{3}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{1}\neq m\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{0}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{0}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
+\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
m_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg(\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{3}}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{3}}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\bigg)\\
=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=m,\\
m_{1}\neq m\end{subarray}}\bigg({f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{2}}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi_{0}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{0}(x_{1})\big]\circ\big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{2}}(x_{2})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\bigg)\\
+\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{3}=m,\\
m_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg(\big[\pi^{\prime}_{0}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{m_{3}}(x_{2})\big]\ -\ \big[\pi^{\prime}_{m_{3}}(x_{1})\big]\circ{f_{\!\!\;m_{1}}}\circ\big[\pi_{0}(x_{2})\big]\bigg)\,.

We thus have proved the existence of
B_{0}
-endomorphisms
{f_{\!\!\;m}}
of
V_{0}

(m\in\mathbb{N})
such that assertion
(\ast_{m})
holds for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
. One then concludes by considering the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear endomorphism
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
of
V_{0}[|h|]
defined by the following:

{f_{\!\!\;h}}(v)\ :=\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{f_{\!\!\;m}}(v)\!\;h^{m}\quad\quad\ (v\in V_{0})\,.

∎

4
Definition of GQE algebras in rank 1

4.1
Non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}

Recall the definition of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
: see subsection
1.3
. The
relations (
13
)
are called the
non-deformable relations

of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

Definition 4.1
.

The algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra generated by
X^{-},X^{+},H
and subject to the non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
:

\big[H,X^{\pm}\big]\ =\ \pm\>\!2\,X^{\pm}\,.

The universal enveloping algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
is the
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-},X^{+},H
and subject to the non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
. We designate also by
H
, when the context makes it unambiguous, the
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism from
\Bbbk[H]
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
which sends
H
to
H
.

A
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
algebra designates a
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-pointed algebra. We regard the algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
as a
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra: the
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-point, which we denote by
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, is the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
.

Definition 4.2
.

We denote by
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
the constant formal deformation of the
{\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}}
-pointed

\Bbbk
-algebra

{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
.

4.2
The global crystal of rank 1

Definition 4.3
.

The global crystal of rank 1, denoted by
\mathbb{B}
, is the quiver defined by the following.

•

The set
B
of vertices of
\mathbb{B}
is
{\big\{b_{n,p}:=(n,n-2p)\;\!;\,n,i\in\mathbb{N},\,p\leq n\big\}}
.

•

The set
E
of edges of
\mathbb{B}
is the disjoint union
{E^{-}\sqcup E^{+}}
of two copies
of
[E]:={\big\{(n,k)\;\!;\,n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\,k\leq n\big\}}
. The source of the edge
(n,k)\in E^{\pm}
is
(n,\pm(n-2k+2))
and its target is
(n,\pm(n-2k))
.

Here is an illustration of the global crystal of rank 1. Remark that there is no arrow for
n=0
.

\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \iddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\iddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{1,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{0,0}}}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \cdots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{1,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \ddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote by
\mathbb{B}_{n}
the connected component of
\mathbb{B}
containing the vertex
b_{n,0}
. We denote by
B_{n}
and
E_{n}
the sets of vertices and edges in
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, respectively. The quiver
\mathbb{B}_{n}
is called the
n
th component of
\mathbb{B}
.

4.3
Colourings

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, remark that the irreducible representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
can be defined as the
\Bbbk
-vector space
{\Bbbk B_{n}}
, where the actions of
X^{\pm},H
are given by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,\\
&&\\
X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}(p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.\\
&&\\
X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}(n-p+1)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}\right.

We regard
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
also as a representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
.

Definition 4.4
.

We denote by
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
the ring
\Bbbk[|h|]^{E}
. An element in
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
is called a colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We denote by
\psi^{\pm}
the restriction of
\psi
to
E^{\pm}
. Here is an illustration of
\psi
restricted to
E_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
):

\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,1)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,n)}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,2)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,n-1)}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,n-1)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,2)}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,n)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,1)}

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote
by
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
the representation
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
whose underlying
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module is
{(\Bbbk B_{n})[|h|]}
and where the actions of
X^{\pm},H
are defined by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,\\
&&\\
X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{-}(n,p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.\\
&&\\
X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{+}(n,n-p+1)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}\right.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. If the restriction of
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero, then the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) is indecomposable.

Proof.

Suppose that the restriction of
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero. Suppose that there exist nonzero subrepresentations
V,V^{\prime}
of
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
such that
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}=V\oplus V^{\prime}
. Remark that the pull-back of
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
via the morphism
H
is isomorphic to a direct sum of one-dimensional irreducible representations of
\Bbbk[H]
. Hence, the
pull-backs
of
V,V^{\prime}
by
H
are also isomorphic to direct sums of one-dimensional representations of
\Bbbk[H]
. As a consequence, there exists
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
such that
b_{n,p}\in V
and
b_{n,p^{\prime}}\in V^{\prime}
. Since the restriction of
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero and in view of the action of
X^{\pm}
on
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
, one then obtains a contradiction.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

•

The colouring
\psi
is said non-degenerate if the function
\psi
has no zero.

•

The colouring
\psi
is said invertible if
\psi
is invertible in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

4.4
Notations and conventions

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras
\Bbbk[|h|]^{[E]}
and
\Bbbk^{[E]}[|h|]
are identified: an element
f\in\Bbbk[|h|]^{[E]}
is identified with the formal power series
{f_{\!\!\;h}}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{f_{\!\!\>m}\>\!h^{m}}
, where
{f_{\!\!\>m}}\in\Bbbk^{[E]}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and such that the following holds:

f(n,k)\ =\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{f_{\!\!\>m}}(n,k)\,h^{m}\,,\quad\quad\forall\,(n,k)\in[E]\,.

Let now
P_{h}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}P_{m}\,h^{m}
be an element in
\Bbbk[u,v][|h|]
and let
n,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. We denote by
P_{h}(n,k)
the power series in
\Bbbk[|h|]
defined by the following:

P_{h}(n,k)\ :=\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}P_{m}(n,k)\,h^{m}\,.

Remark that a polynomial in
\Bbbk[u,v]
is zero if and only if its evaluations at
(n,k)
are zero for all
(n,k)\in[E]
. We then regard the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
\Bbbk[u,v][|h|]
as a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-subalgebra
of
\Bbbk[|h|]^{[E]}
: an element
P_{h}\in\Bbbk[u,v][|h|]
is identified with the function in
\Bbbk[|h|]^{[E]}
whose value
(n,k)
is
P_{h}(n,k)
for every
(n,k)\in[E]
.

Let
f\in\Bbbk[|h|]^{[E]}
and let
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k\leq n
. We denote by
f(n,k)!
the power series defined by the following:

f(n,k)!\ :=\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\prod_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}f(n,k^{\prime})&\quad\ \text{if }k\geq 1\,,\\
0&\quad\ \text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

4.5
The algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

Definition 4.8
.

•

We denote by
I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)
the two-sided ideal of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
formed by the elements acting by zero on all the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

(n\in\mathbb{N})
.

•

We denote by
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
the quotient of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
by
I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)
.

We denote by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
with the
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-point
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}\circ\iota
, where
\iota
designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
.

Remark 4.9
.

We regard
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) also as a representation of the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. By definition then, an element in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

(n\in\mathbb{N})
.

The algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is built from the topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
and
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) and therefore inherits from them the property.

Lemma 4.10
.

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is topologically free.

Proof.

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
is by definition topologically free. It is in particular complete. Being a quotient of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is thus also complete.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
A_{n}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra

{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk[|h|]}}({L_{h}(n,\psi)})
and we denote by
f_{n}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
A_{n}
, associated with the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
. We denote then by
f
the product of the morphisms
f_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
), it is a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

to
\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A_{n}
. The definition of the ideal
I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)
can be rephrased by the following equation:

I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)\ =\ \ker(f)\,.

(15)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is by definition topologically free. It is in particular separated and torsion-free. Hence, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A_{n}
is also
separated
and torsion-free. Besides, in view of equation (
15
), one can identify the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
with a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule of
\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A_{n}
. As a consequence, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is
separated
and torsion-free.

To sum up, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is separated,
complete
and torsion-free. It is thus topologically free (see for example
[
28
, proposition XVI.2.4]
).
∎

4.6
Congruence

Definition 4.11
.

•

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The congruent class
[\psi]

of
\psi
is the function from
[E]

to
\Bbbk[|h|]
defined by the following:

[\psi](n,k)\ :=\ \psi^{-}(n,k)\ \psi^{+}(n,n-k+1)\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

•

Two colourings
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
are said congruent if
[\psi_{1}]=[\psi_{2}]
. We denote by
\equiv
the congruence relation on
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

The following lemma gives an interpretation of the congruence relation.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be two admissible colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

1)

If
\psi_{1}\equiv\psi_{2}
, then the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
are isomorphic.

2)

We assume that
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
divide each other in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are congruent.

(ii)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
and
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{2})}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

Let
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
be two admissible colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
such that
\psi^{-}
divide
(\psi^{\prime})^{-}
in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. Let us first prove the following assertion.

The colourings
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are congruent if and only if there exists an injective
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(
\ast
)

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
H.b_{n,p}={(n-2p)\!\>b_{n,p}}
in both
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, a
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
must send, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, the vector
b_{n,p}
to a scalar multiple of it. Since besides the vector
b_{n,p}
is a scalar multiple of
(X^{-})^{p}.b_{n,0}
in both
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, a
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
is thus a scalar multiple of the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear endomorphism
{f_{\!\!\>n}}
of
{(\Bbbk B_{n})[|h|]}
, defined by the following:

f_{\!\!\>n}(b_{n,p})\ :=\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p)!}\ b_{n,p}\quad\quad\ (p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n})\,.

Remark that since the colouring
\psi^{\prime}
is admissible, the map
{f_{\!\!\>n}}
is injective. Remark then that for every
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq n}
, the following equalities hold in
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
, respectively:

\displaystyle f_{\!\!\>n}\big(X^{+}.\!\>b_{n,p}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi^{+}(n,n-p+1)\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p-1)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p-1)!}\ b_{n,p}\,,

\displaystyle X^{+}.\big(f_{\!\!\>n}(b_{n,p})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi^{\prime})^{+}(n,n-p+1)\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p)!}\ b_{n,p}\,.

Assertion
(\ast)
follows. Point 2 of the lemma is a consequence. Let us now prove point 1. Denote by
\psi
the colouring of
\mathbb{B}
defined by the following

\psi^{-}(n,k)\ :=\ 1\,,\quad\quad\psi^{+}(n,k)\ :=\ [\psi_{1}](n,k)\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

Remark that
\psi\equiv\psi_{1}
. Assertion
(\ast)
then implies that there exists an injective
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. As a consequence, in view of the definition of the algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
, there exists an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra between the two latter. One concludes.
∎

4.7
h
-admissible colourings

Definition 4.13
.

A colouring
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
is said
h
-admissible if it satisfies the following axioms.

\displaystyle\bullet\text{ (Deformation axiom)}

\displaystyle{[\psi]}_{0}(n,k)\,=\,k\!\;(n-k+1)\,,\ \ \forall\,(n,k)\in[E]\,.

\displaystyle\bullet\text{ (Regularity axiom)}

\displaystyle[\psi]\,\in\,\Bbbk[u,v][|h|]\,.

\displaystyle\bullet\text{ (Quotient axiom)}

\displaystyle[\psi](n,n+1)\,=\,0\,,\ \ \forall\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,.

\displaystyle\bullet\text{ (Verma axiom)}

\displaystyle[\psi](-n-2,k)\ =\ [\psi](n,n+k+1)\,,\ \ \forall\,n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}.

Note, in view of the deformation axiom, that a
h
-admissible colouring is in particular admissible.

Remark 4.14
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
such that
\psi^{-}=\psi^{+}
and which depends only on the second variable:
\psi^{\pm}(n,k)=\psi^{\pm}(n^{\prime},k)
for all
n,n^{\prime},k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
such
that
k\leq n,n^{\prime}
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, we denote by
\psi(k)
the common value of
\psi^{-},\psi^{+}
at
(n,k)

with
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq k}
and we regard
\psi
as a function from
[E]
to
\Bbbk[|h|]
. The
colouring
\psi
is
h
-admissible if and only if the following conditions hold:

\psi\,\in\,\Bbbk[v][|h|]\,,\quad\quad\psi(-v)\,=\,-\,\psi(v)\,,\quad\quad\psi_{0}(v)\,=\,\pm\!\;v\,.

(16)

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be two
h
-admissible colourings such that
\psi_{1}^{-}=\psi_{1}^{+}
,
\psi_{2}^{-}=\psi_{2}^{+}
and which depend only on the second variable. The colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are congruent if and only if
\psi_{1}=\pm\;\!\psi_{2}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\psi
be colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
if and only if
{[\psi]}_{0}(n,k)={k\!\>(n-k+1)}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq n}
.

Proof.

Denote by
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}_{0}
the pull-back of
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}_{|h=0}
by the unique morphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}_{|h=0}
. Denote then by
g_{n}
the
\Bbbk
-linear endomorphism of
{\Bbbk B_{n}}
, defined by the following:

g_{n}(b_{n,p})\ :=\ \frac{{(\psi^{-})}_{0}(n,p)!}{p\!\>!}\ b_{n,p}\quad\quad\ (p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq p})\,.

Suppose that
{[\psi]}_{0}(n,k)={k\!\>(n-k+1)}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq n}
. The map
g_{n}
then is a
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-isomorphism from
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}_{0}
. Remark besides that the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is by definition topologically free. The representation is therefore
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
.
