Let us prove the converse implication. Let us then suppose that there exists a
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-isomorphism
g^{\prime}_{n}
from
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}_{0}
. The vector
b_{n,p}
being of weight
n-2p
in both
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}_{0}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, the map
g^{\prime}_{n}
must send, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, the vector
b_{n,p}
to a (nonzero) scalar multiple of it. By considering the actions of
X^{+}X^{-}
on the two representations, one concludes.
∎

The following definition will be justified a posteriori by theorem
5.1
.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. The
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra

{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is called the generalised quantum enveloping algebra of rank 1 associated to
\psi
.

5
Main results in rank 1

5.1
Formal deformation

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The following assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The colouring
\psi
is
h
-admissible.

(ii)

The
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
.

Proof.

Claim 1
.

If assertion (ii) holds, then
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The following equalities hold in the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
:

\displaystyle\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(H).\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,

\displaystyle\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{+}X^{-}).\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[\psi]}_{0}(n,p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{+}X^{-}).\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[\psi]}_{0}(n,p)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

Suppose that assertion (ii) holds. The following relation then holds in the specialisation
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
:

\Big[\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{+}),\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{-})\Big]\ =\ \pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(H)\,.

(19)

As a consequence, the following equalities hold:

{[\psi]}_{0}(n,1)\ =\ n\,,\quad\quad\ {[\psi]}_{0}(n,p+1)\ =\ {[\psi]}_{0}(n,p)\,+\,(n-2p)\,,\quad\forall\,p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq n}\,.

One concludes.
∎

Claim 2
.

We assume that the colouring
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom. The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The colouring
\psi
is
h
-admissible.

ii’)

The relation (
19
) holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\psi
is
h
-admissible. There then exists a solution
M
of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
: see theorem
A.6
. This is turn implies that the following relation holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
(see proposition
A.2
):

X^{+}X^{-}\ =\ \sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,{(X^{-})}^{a}\!\;M_{a}(H)\!\;{(X^{+})}^{a}\,.

(20)

Besides, since
\psi
satisfies by assumption the deformation axiom, the following equalities hold in
\Bbbk[u]
(see proposition
A.5
):

\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}(M_{0})\ =\ u\,,\quad\ \pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}(M_{1})\ =\ 1\,,\quad\ \pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}(M_{p})\ =\ u\,,\ \ \forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}\,.

Assertion ii’) follows.

Let us prove the converse. Denote by
T
the subset
{\{(X^{-})^{a}H^{b}(X^{+})^{c}\;\!;\,a,b,c\in\mathbb{N}\}}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and denote by
f
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-linear map from
{(\Bbbk\!\;T)[|h|]}

to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
induced by the inclusion map from
T
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
(note that the map
f
exists since the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is separated and complete: we know from lemma
4.10
that it is topologically free). The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed, the following relations hold in the specialisation
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
:

\Big[\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(H),\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{\pm})\Big]\ =\ \pm\>\!2\ \pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{\pm})\,.

Remark besides that the
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
is generated by
\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(X^{\pm})
and
\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(H)
. Suppose now that assertion ii’) holds. It follows that the
\Bbbk
-vector
space
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
is then spanned by
\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}(T)
. In other words, the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
f
is surjective. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being separated and complete, the map
f
is thus also surjective. There exists in particular a
sequence
{(M_{a})}_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
, which converges to zero and such that the relation (
20
) holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. This implies that the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
admits a solution and that
\psi
is therefore
h
-admissible: see again proposition
A.2
and theorem
A.6
, respectively.
∎

Suppose that assertion (ii) holds. The relation (
19
) then holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
. Assertion (i) follows from claims
1
and
2
. Let us prove the reverse implication.

Let
x_{h}
be an element in the ideal
I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)

of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
. Denote by
\bar{x}_{0}
the image
in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
of
x_{0}\in{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
by
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
. Since the colouring
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom, the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
of the pull-back of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}

via
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
x_{h}\in I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)
, the element
\bar{x}_{0}\in{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
then acts as zero on all the representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}

with
n\in\mathbb{N}
and is thus zero: see subsection
1.5
. As a consequence, there exists a
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism
f_{0}
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}\ }
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}}

Suppose now that
\psi
is
h
-admissible. We know from claim
2
that the
relation (
19
)
then holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
. As a consequence, and in view of the presentation of the algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
(see subsection
1.3
), there exists a
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism
g_{0}
from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
which makes the following diagram commute

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}\ }
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{0}}

Since the maps
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
and
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
are surjective, the morphisms
g_{0}\circ f_{0}
and
f_{0}\circ g_{0}
are therefore equal to the identity maps. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being topologically free (see
lemma
4.10
), one concludes.
∎

5.2
Classical and standard quantum realisations

Let us recall the definition of the
formal quantum algebra

{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
associated to the classical Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
. The following elements are defined in the power series ring
\Bbbk[|h|]
:

q\ :=\ \exp(h)\,,\quad\quad\quad{[k]}_{q}\ :=\ \frac{q^{k}-q^{-k}}{q-q^{-1}}\quad\ (k\in\mathbb{Z})\,.

The formal quantum algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
is the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra topologically generated by
X^{-},X^{+},H
and subject to the following relations:

\displaystyle\big[H,X^{\pm}\big]

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle\pm\;\!2\,X^{\pm}\,,

\displaystyle\big[X^{+},X^{-}\big]

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle\frac{q^{H}-q^{-H}}{q-q^{-1}}\,.

We denote by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, which sends
X^{\pm}
to
X^{\pm}

and
H
to
H
. We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
with the
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-point
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}\circ\iota
, where
\iota
designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
.

Let us now recall the description of the fundamental representations of the quantum algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{h}(n)}
can be defined as the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{(\Bbbk B_{n})[|h|]}
, where the actions of
X^{\pm},H
are given by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\displaystyle H.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,

\displaystyle X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[p+1]}_{q}\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[n-p+1]}_{q}\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

The
classical colouring
and the
quantum colouring
, denoted by
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
and
{\psi_{\!\!\;q}}
, respectively, are the colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
, defined by the following:

(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ k\,,\quad\quad({\psi_{\!\!\;q}})^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ {[k]}_{q}\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

Note, in view of remark
4.14
, that the colourings
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
and
{\psi_{\!\!\;q}}
are
h
-admissible
.

Proposition 5.2
.

1)

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and the constant formal deformation
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras.

2)

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},{\psi_{\!\!\;q}})}
and the formal quantum algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras.

Proof.

Remark that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
the pull-back of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}[|h|]
by
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
is the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
. As a consequence, there exists a morphism
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ {\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]\,}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{f_{\!\!\;h}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}}

Since
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
is a formal deformation of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
(see theorem
5.1
), the map
({f_{\!\!\;h}})_{|h=0}
is bijective. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being topologically free, the map
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
is then also bijective. In other words, the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
are isomorphic. The proof in the quantum case is similar: remark first that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
the pull-back of the representation
{L_{h}(n)}
by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
is the representation
{L_{h}(n,{\psi_{\!\!\;q}})}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
and remark that the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}_{|h=0},{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
are isomorphic. The proof in the quantum case is analog.
∎

5.3
Presentation by generators and relations

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a
h
-admissible colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. We denote by
{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}={({S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,p}})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
the solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
: see proposition
A.4
and
theorem
A.6
. We denote by
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
topologically generated by
X^{-},X^{+},H
and subject to the following relations:

\displaystyle\big[H,X^{\pm}\big]

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle 2\>\!X^{\pm}\,,

\displaystyle X^{+}X^{-}

\displaystyle\,=\

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,{(X^{-})}^{a}\>\!{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H)\;\!{(X^{+})}^{a}\,.

Note that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H){(X^{+})}^{a}
converges since the
sequence
{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}
converges to zero. The projection map from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
endows the latter with a structure of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
.

Theorem 5.3
.

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is isomorphic to the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

Proof.

Since
{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
and since the colouring
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom, the following equalities hold in
\Bbbk[u]
(see proposition
A.5
):

\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,0}})\ =\ u\,,\quad\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,1}})\ =\ 1\,,\quad\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,p}})\ =\ u\,,\ \,\forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}\,.

As a consequence, the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebras
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
and
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
are isomorphic (recall the presentation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
: see subsection
1.3
). Besides, since
{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
, the following relation holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
(see proposition
A.2
):

X^{+}X^{-}\ =\ \sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,{(X^{-})}^{a}\!\;{S^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H)\!\;{(X^{+})}^{a}\,.

Hence, there exists a morphism
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra from
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
(the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is separated and complete). On the one hand, we know that
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed

\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
(see theorem
5.1
). On the other hand, we have proved that the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebras
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
and
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}_{|h=0}
are isomorphic. As a consequence, the map
({f_{\!\!\;h}})_{|h=0}
is bijective. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being complete and separated, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being separated and torsion-free, the map
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
is then bijective.
∎

5.4
Integrable representations

Let
\psi
be a colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
. We denote by
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
the category of integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which are topologically free when viewed as
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible colouring of
\mathbb{B}
.

1)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

2)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is, up to isomorphism, equal to the representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
topologically generated by
v
and subject to the following relations:

H.\!\>v\ =\ n\;\!v\,,\quad\quad X^{+}.\!\>v\ =\ 0\,,\quad\quad(X^{-})^{n+1}.\!\>v\ =\ 0\,.

3)

The representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) are pairwise distinct and are the unique, up to isomorphism, indecomposable representations in
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

4)

A representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is in the category
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
if and only if it is
\psi
-integrable.

5)

Let
n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. Every
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n^{\prime},\psi)}
is equal to a scalar multiple of the identity map if
n=n^{\prime}
and is zero else.

6)

Every integrable representation of the algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
admits a unique, up to isomorphism, formal deformation along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

Proof.

Denote by
f_{0}
the unique morphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Recall besides
lemma
4.15
: since
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom, the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

According to the definition of
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
, there exists a
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
-morphism
g_{h}
from
{L^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
. Besides, according to the definition of the representation
{L^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(n,\psi)}
, the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
is isomorphic to the pull-back of
{L^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(n,\psi)}_{|h=0}
via
f_{0}
(recall the presentation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
: see subsection
1.5
). On the other hand, we have proved that
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. Hence, the map
(g_{h})_{|h=0}
is bijective. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{L^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(n,\psi)}
being complete and separated, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
being separated and torsion-free, the map
g_{h}
is then bijective.

Remark that the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
is integrable. We besides now from lemma
4.6
that the representation is indecomposable. Let now
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n_{1}\neq n_{2}
. Remark that the representations
{L_{h}(n_{1},\psi)}
and
{L_{h}(n_{2},\psi)}
are not isomorphic since the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules
{L_{h}(n_{1},\psi)}
and
{L_{h}(n_{2},\psi)}
are not.

Let
V_{0}
be an integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
and let
{V_{\!\!\;h}},{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}}
be two formal deformations of
V_{0}
along
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. Let
W_{0}
be an extension of
V_{0}
by
V_{0}
.
Since
V_{0}
is an integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
on
W_{0}
are locally finite and the representation
W_{0}
is then isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
(see proposition
1.1
). The representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) being irreducible, the representation
W_{0}
is in particular semisimple and hence a trivial extension of
V_{0}
by
V_{0}
. This being true for every extension
W_{0}
of
V_{0}
by
V_{0}
, the representations
{V_{\!\!\;h}}

and
{V^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}}
are thus isomorphic (see proposition
3.5
). As a consequence, and since again every integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
(see proposition
1.1
), there exists for every integrable representation
V_{0}
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, a unique, up to isomorphism, formal deformation of
V_{0}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
, which besides is isomorphic to a topologically direct sums of representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since besides every integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is a formal deformation along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
of an integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, point 3 of the theorem follows and every indecomposable integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is then isomorphic to some representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

5.5
H
-triviality

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a
h
-admissible colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. Let
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}={({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,p}})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
be the solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
: see proposition
A.4
and theorem
A.6
.

We recall that
H
designates also the
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism from
\Bbbk[H]
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
which sends
H
to
H
.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
. There exists a
{H\!\!\>}
-trivialisation
\gamma_{h}
of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which satisfies the following:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\gamma_{h}(X^{-})&=&X^{-}\,,\\
\gamma_{h}(H)&=&H\,,\\
\gamma_{h}(X^{+})&=&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\!\>{(X^{-})}^{a}\>\!{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H)\;\!{(X^{+})}^{a+1}\,.\end{array}\right.

Note that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}
converges since the
sequence
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}
converges to zero.

Proof.

Let us prove the existence of a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which satisfies the following:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\gamma^{\prime}_{h}(X^{-})&=&X^{-}\,,\\
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}(H)&=&H\,,\\
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}(X^{+})&=&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\!\>{(X^{-})}^{a}\>\!{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H)\;\!{(X^{+})}^{a+1}\,.\end{array}\right.

(23)

The theorem will follows since the GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and the constant formal deformation
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras (see proposition
5.2
).
Since the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
depend only on the congruence class of
\psi
(see lemma
4.12
concerning
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
), one can without loss of generality assume that
\psi
has values in
\Bbbk[|h|]^{\times}
(remark that
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom). Since
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h}}
is a the solution of
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
, there then exists a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which satisfies the conditions (
23
): see proposition
A.3
. Besides, since the colouring
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom, the following equalities hold
in
\Bbbk[u]
(see proposition
A.5
):

\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,0}})\ =\ 0\,,\quad\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,1}})\ =\ 1\,,\quad\pi_{|h=0}^{\Bbbk[u][|h|]}({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi}_{\!\!\;h,p}})\ =\ 0\,,\ \,\forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}\,.

As a consequence the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}
is a morphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra between
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. The latter algebras being both generated by
X^{\pm}
and
H
, the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}
is therefore bijective. The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
being both topologically free, the map
\gamma^{\prime}_{h}
is thus also bijective. We are done.
∎

5.6
Classification

Theorem 5.6
.

The following map is a bijection:

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$h$-admissible}\\
\text{colourings of $\mathbb{B}$,}\\
\text{up to congruence}\end{array}}\right\}

\displaystyle\ \longrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{${H\!\!\>}$-trivial formal deformations}\\
\text{of the $\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}$-pointed $\Bbbk$-algebra ${U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}$},\\
\text{up to isomorphism}\\
\text{of $\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}$-pointed $\Bbbk[|h|]$-algebra}\end{array}}\right\}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\text{congruence}\\
\text{class of $\psi$}\end{array}

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\text{isomorphism}\\
\text{class of ${U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}$.}\end{array}

Remark that the map of theorem
5.6
is well defined since GQE algebras associated to congruent colourings are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras: see lemma
4.12
.

Proof.

Let
A_{h}
be a
H
-trivial formal deformation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
. We know from proposition
5.2
that the constant formal deformation
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}[|h|]
and the GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras. Hence, there exists a morphism
\gamma_{h}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra from
A_{h}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
. Denote then by
V_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) the pull-back via
\gamma_{h}
of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
. Since the morphism
\gamma_{h}
sends
H\in A_{h}
to
H\in{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
, there exists
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
such that, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H\in A_{h}
on the representation
V_{n}
are given by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\displaystyle H.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,

\displaystyle X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{-}(n,p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{+}(n,n-p+1)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

Denote now by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
A_{h}
which makes the following diagram commutes, where
\iota
designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
(the morphism
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}}
exists since the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
A_{h}
is complete and separated):

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\dot{A}_{h}}
\textstyle{\,A_{h}}

In view of the definition of the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
(see remark
4.9
), an element in the algebra
A_{h}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all
V_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
). As a consequence, there exists an injective morphism
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
to
A_{h}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{f_{\!\!\;h}}}
\textstyle{A_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{h}}

Since the map
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
is surjective, so then is the map
{f_{\!\!\;h}}
. We thus have proved that the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and
A_{h}
are isomorphic. Since
A_{h}
is a formal deformation of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, so then is
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. Therefore, the colouring
\psi
is
h
-admissible: see theorem
5.1
.

Let now
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
be two
h
-admissible colourings such that the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras

{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
are isomorphic. Let us prove that the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are congruent. Since
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
depend only on the congruent classes of
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, respectively (see
lemma
4.12
), one can assume without of generality that
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
divide each other in the
ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
(remark that the two colourings satisfy the deformation axiom). Denote by
g_{h}
an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
. For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the pull-back of
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{2})}
via
g_{h}
is an integrable and indecomposable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and is thus, in view of theorem
5.4
, isomorphic to the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{1})}
(the ranks of the free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{1})}
and
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{2})}
are equal). As a consequence, the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are congruent: see lemma
4.12
.
∎

6
Definition of GQE algebras

6.1
Non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{g}

Recall the definition of the Kac-Moody algebra
\mathfrak{g}
: see subsection
1.2
. The
relations (
10
)
are called the
non-deformable relations

of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Definition 6.1
.

The algebra
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra generated by
X^{\pm}_{i}
(
i\in I
)
, the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
and subject to the non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{g}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rclr}\big[\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\big]&=&0&(\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[{\check{\mu}},X^{\pm}_{i}\big]&=&\pm\;\!\langle{\check{\mu}},\alpha_{i}\rangle\,X_{i}^{\pm}&(i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{j}\big]&=&0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

The universal enveloping algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
is the
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{\pm}_{i}
(
i\in I
)
, the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
and subject to the non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{g}
. We denote by
\Bbbk[Y]
the symmetric algebra on the
\Bbbk
-vector space
Y\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Bbbk
. We designate also by
Y
, when the context makes it unambiguous, the
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism from
\Bbbk[Y]
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
which sends
\check{\mu}
to
\check{\mu}
for every
\check{\mu}\in Y
.

A
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
algebra designates a
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
-pointed algebra. We regard the algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
as a
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra: the
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
-point, which we denote by
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
, is the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Definition 6.2
.

We denote by
{U_{\!\!\>h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
the constant formal deformation of the
{{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}}
-pointed

\Bbbk
-algebra

{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
.

6.2
\psi
-integrable representations

Definition 6.3
.

We denote by
{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
the ring of functions from
I
to
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. An element
\psi={(\psi_{i})}_{i\in I}
in
{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
is called an
{I\!\!\>}
-colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
, it is said admissible if the colouring
\psi_{i}
is for every
i\in I
.

Let
i\in I
. We denote by
{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}[|h|]
. It is a morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
.

Definition 6.4
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. A representation
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
is said
\psi
-integrable
if the following two conditions are satisfied.

a)

The action of every element
\check{\mu}\in Y
on
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
is diagonalisable.

b)

For every
i\in I
, the pull-back of
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
via
{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is isomorphic to a topologically direct sum of representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{i})}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Note, in view of condition b) in the previous definition, that the
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
are topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules.

6.3
The algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}

In this subsection,
\psi={(\psi_{i})}_{i\in I}
designates a
I
-colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

Definition 6.5
.

•

We denote by
I_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
the two-sided ideal of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
formed by the elements acting by zero on all the
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
.

•

We denote by
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the quotient of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
by
I_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
.

Note that when
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
, the previous definition is equivalent to the one given in rank one: see definition
4.8
.

We denote by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
with the
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
-point
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\circ\iota
, where
\iota
designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
.

Remark 6.6
.

We regard the
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
also as representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. By definition then, an element in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the
\psi
-integrable representations.

The algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is built from the
\psi
-integrable representations, which are topologically free
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules, and therefore inherits from them the property.

Proposition 6.7
.

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is topologically free.

Proof.

The
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
is by definition topologically free. It is in particular complete. Being a quotient of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is thus also complete.

Let us denote by
\mathrm{Int}(\psi)
the class of
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
. Let now
V
be a
\psi
-integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
, we denote by
A_{V}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra

{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk[|h|]}}(V)
and we denote by
f_{V}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
A_{V}
, associated with the representation
V
. We denote by
f
the product of the morphisms
f_{V}
(
V\in\mathrm{Int}(\psi)
), it is a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}

to
\prod_{V\in\mathrm{Int}(\psi)}A_{V}
. The definition of
I_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
can be rephrased by the following equation:

I_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)\ =\ \ker(f)\,.

(28)

Let
V
be a
\psi
-integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
V
is by definition topologically free. It is in particular separated and torsion-free. Hence, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
\prod_{V\in\mathrm{Int}(\psi)}A_{V}
is also
separated
and torsion-free. Besides, in view of equation (
28
), one can identify
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
with a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule
of
\prod_{V\in\mathrm{Int}(\psi)}A_{V}
. As a consequence, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is
separated
and torsion-free.

To sum up, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is separated,
complete
and torsion-free. It is thus topologically free (see for example
[
28
, proposition XVI.2.4]
).
∎

6.4
The diagram
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}

In this subsection,
\psi={(\psi_{i})}_{i\in I}
designates a
I
-colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

We denote by
\mathbf{I}
the category defined by the following.

•

The objects of
\mathbf{I}
are the elements of the Dynkin
set
I
together with an additional object
t_{I}
.

•

The morphisms of
\mathbf{I}
are the identity morphisms, together with a morphism denoted
\Delta_{i}
from
i
to
t_{I}
, for each
i\in I
.

Let
i\in I
, we denote by
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
the
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
which
sends
X^{\pm}
to
X_{i}^{\pm}
and
H
to
\check{\alpha}_{i}
.

Definition 6.8
.

We denote by
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
the
\Bbbk
-diagram of shape
\mathbf{I}
defined by the following:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}(i)

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

(i
∈
I)   ,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}(t_{I})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}(\Delta_{i})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}

(i
∈
I)   .

Thanks to the simple
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-directions (see subsection
1.3
), we also endow the algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
with a structure of
\Bbbk
-diagram.

Definition 6.9
.

We denote by
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the
\Bbbk
-diagram of shape
\mathbf{I}
, defined by the following:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}(i)

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}

(i
∈
I)   ,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}(t_{I})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}(\Delta_{i})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}

(i
∈
I)   .

A
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed diagram
designates a
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
-pointed diagram. We regard the diagram
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
as a
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-diagram, whose
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
-point
{\Pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is defined by the following:

\displaystyle{({\Pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})})}_{i}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}\quad\ (i\in I)\,,

\displaystyle{({\Pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})})}_{t_{I}}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,.

Definition-proposition 6.10
.

Let
i\in I
. We denote by
{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the unique
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ }
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}\ }
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ {\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{\ {U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\,.}

(29)

Proof.

Let
x\in I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})
and denote by
y
the image of
x
by
{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
.
Let
V
be a
\psi
-integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
. By definition, the pull-back of
V
via the morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{i})}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
x
belongs to the ideal
I_{h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})
, the element
y
than acts as zero on
V
. This being true for every
\psi
-integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
, the image of
y
by
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is zero. Hence, the map
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\circ{\Delta^{i}_{h}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
can be factorised through
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
, i.e. there exists a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which makes the diagram (
29
) commutes. The unicity part of the proposition follows from the surjectivity of
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
.
∎

Thanks to the morphisms
{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
(
i\in I
), we endow the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
with a structure of diagram.

Definition 6.11
.

We denote by
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the
\Bbbk
-diagram of shape
\mathbf{I}
, defined by the following:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}(i)

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}

(i
∈
I)   ,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}(t_{I})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}(\Delta_{i})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}

(i
∈
I)   .

We endow the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
with the
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
-point
{\Pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
, defined by the following:

\displaystyle{({\Pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)})}_{i}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}\,\circ\,\iota_{i}\quad\ (i\in I)\,,

\displaystyle{({\Pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)})}_{t_{I}}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\,\circ\,\iota\,,

where
\iota_{i}
(
i\in I
) designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
and
where
\iota
designates the inclusion map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
.

6.5
h
-admissible
I
-colourings

Definition 6.12
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The
{I\!\!\;}
-colouring
\psi
is said
h
-admissible if the
colouring
\psi_{i}
is
h
-admissible for every
i\in I
.

The following definition will be justified a posteriori by theorem
9.1
.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible
{I\!\!\;}
-colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
. The
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra

{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is called the generalised quantum enveloping algebra associated to
\psi
.

7
Classical and standard quantum realisations

7.1
Standard quantum algebras

Let
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and let
i\in I
. The following elements are defined in the ring
\Bbbk[|h|]
, where we recall that
q
designates the formal power series
\exp(h)
:

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle q^{d_{i}}\,,

[k]_q_i

:=

q

i

k

- q

i

-k

q

i

-1

,

\displaystyle{[k]}_{q_{i}}!

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\prod_{k^{\prime}=0}^{k}\,{[k^{\prime}]}_{q_{i}}\ ,

[

k

k’

]
_q_i

:=

[k+k’]

q

[k]

⋅

The
(formal) standard quantum algebra

{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g})}
is the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra topologically generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the non-deformable relations (
10
) of
\mathfrak{g}
and to the following relations:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle{\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]}=\displaystyle{\frac{K_{i}-K_{i}^{-1}}{q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1}}}\,,\quad\quad\text{where }\,K_{i}:=\exp(d_{i}\>\!h\;\!{\check{\alpha}_{i}})&(i\in I)\,,\\
\displaystyle{\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\brack k}_{q_{i}}\,{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}\;\!X^{\pm}_{j}\;\!{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}}=\,\displaystyle 0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

7.2
Theorem

Recall the definition of the classical and quantum colouring of
\mathbb{B}
:

(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ k\,,\quad\quad({\psi_{\!\!\;q}})^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ {[k]}_{q}\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

We denote by
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
and
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}}
the
h
-admissible
I
-colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
defined by the following:

{({\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}_{i}\ :=\ \psi_{\mathrm{cl}}\,,\quad\quad{({\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}})}_{i}\ :=\ \psi_{q_{i}}\quad\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

Theorem 7.1
.

1)

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}
and the constant formal deformation
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}[|h|]
are isomorphic
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras.

2)

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}})}
and the standard quantum algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g})}
are isomorphic
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebras.

Proof.

The proof is analog to the one given in rank 1(see proposition
5.2
) and makes use of proposition
1.2
.
∎

8
GQE algebras without Serre relations

8.1
Definition

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. Let
i\in I
, we denote by
{S^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h}}={({S^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,p}})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
the solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{i},\psi_{i})}
: see proposition
A.4
and
theorem
A.6
.

Definition 8.1
.

The GQE algebra without Serre relations associated to
\psi
, denoted by
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
, is the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra topologically generated by
X^{\pm}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the non-deformable relations (
10
) of
\mathfrak{g}
and to the following relations:

\big[X_{i}^{+},X_{i}^{-}\big]\ =\ \sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,{(X_{i}^{-})}^{a}\!\;{S^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(\check{\alpha}_{i})\!\;{(X_{i}^{+})}^{a}\quad\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

(30)

Note that the series in the relations (
30
) converge since the
sequences
{S^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h}}
converge to zero for all
i\in I
.

We denote by
\tilde{\pi}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
, and we endow the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
with a structure of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra thanks to
\tilde{\pi}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
.

Definition-proposition 8.2
.

There exists a unique and surjective morphism of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. We denote it by
\varpi_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)
.

Proof.

Thanks to the morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
(
i\in I
) and in view of theorem
5.3
, remark that the relations (
30
) hold in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.
∎

Proposition
8.2
asserts that the following diagram commutes, and thus presents a hierarchy between the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
,
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\pi}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{\,{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\varpi_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
\textstyle{\,{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\,.}

(31)

8.2
Formal deformations

It is possible to give different proofs of the theorem below. We have chosen here to use the diamond lemma. The reader is referred to
[
2
]
where the lemma is originally proved and to
[
22
]
for an extension in the
h
-adic case. We use the same terminology than the one employed in the two references.

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
\psi={(\psi_{i})}_{i\in I}
be a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring. The
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
.

Proof.

Pick a family
{(\check{\mu}_{k})}_{1\leq k\leq r}
with values in
Y
which contains the linearly independent family
{(\check{\alpha}_{i})}_{i\in I}
and such that the induced family
{(\check{\mu}_{k}\otimes 1)}_{1\leq k\leq r}
is a basis of the
\Bbbk
-vector space
Y\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Bbbk
.

Denote by
\Lambda
the set
\bigcup_{i\in I}\{X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}\}\cup{\{\check{\mu}_{k}\;\!;\,1\leq k\leq r\}}
and denote by
\prec
the partial order on
\Lambda
defined by the following:

\check{\mu}_{k}\,\prec\,X^{-}_{i}\,\prec\,X^{+}_{j}\quad(i,j\in I,\ 1\leq k\leq r)\quad\text{ and }\quad\check{\mu}_{k}\,\prec\,\check{\mu}_{k^{\prime}}\quad(1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq r)\,.

Let
M=x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{l}
be a monomial in
\langle\Lambda\rangle
(with
l\in\mathbb{N}
). The number
\mathrm{Inv}(M)
of inversions of
M
and the length
\mathrm{lg}(M)
of
M
are defined by the following:

\mathrm{Inv}(M)\ :=\ \#\Big\{(s,t)\,;\ s<t\text{ and }x_{s}\succ x_{t}\Big\}\,,\quad\ \mathrm{lg}(M)\ :=\ l\,.

The partial order
\prec
on
\Lambda
induces a semigroup partial order on
\Bbbk\langle\Lambda\rangle
again denoted
by
\prec
and defined by the following for
M,M^{\prime}
two monomials in
\langle\Lambda\rangle
:

M\,\prec\,M^{\prime}\quad\text{if}\quad\big(\mathrm{Inv}(M),\,\mathrm{lg}(M)\big)\,<_{\>\!\text{lexicographic}}\,\big(\mathrm{Inv}(M^{\prime}),\,\mathrm{lg}(M^{\prime})\big)\,.

We define now a reduction system
\mathcal{S}
, compatible with the semigroup partial order
\prec
previously defined and accordingly to the non-deformable relations (
10
) of
\mathfrak{g}
and to the relations (
30
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\big(\check{\mu}_{k^{\prime}}\;\!\check{\mu}_{k}\,,\,\check{\mu}_{k}\;\!\check{\mu}_{k^{\prime}}\big)\,;\ 1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq r\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\big(X_{i}^{\pm}\>\!\check{\mu}_{k}\,,\,\check{\mu}_{k}\>\!X^{\pm}_{i}\;\!\mp\;\!\langle\check{\mu}_{k},\alpha_{i}\rangle\>\!X^{\pm}_{i}\big)\,;\ i\in I,\ 1\leq k\leq r\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\big(X_{j}^{+}\>\!X^{-}_{i}\,,\,X^{-}_{i}\>\!X_{j}^{+}\big)\,;\ i,j\in I,\ i\neq j\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\big(X_{i}^{+}\>\!X^{-}_{i}\,,\,X^{-}_{i}\>\!X_{i}^{+}\,+\,\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,P_{a}^{\>\!\psi_{i},\psi_{i}}(\check{\alpha}_{i})\left(X_{i}^{-}\right)^{a}\left(X_{i}^{+}\right)^{a}\big)\,;\ i\in I\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}\,\sqcup\,\mathcal{S}_{2}\,\sqcup\,\mathcal{S}_{3}\,\sqcup\,\mathcal{S}_{4}\,.

Let us now prove that the ambiguities of
\mathcal{S}
are resolvable. We treat both the inclusion ambiguities and the overlap ambiguities. The proofs of resolvability are given below in terms of commutative diagrams, where the arrows stand for the reduction maps of
\Bbbk\langle\Lambda\rangle
induced by
\mathcal{S}
. The symbol ‘
\infty

h
-adic topology. Let
1\leq k\leq r
and let
i\in I
, we denote by
a_{ki}
the number
\langle\check{\mu}_{k},\alpha_{i}\rangle
.

\bullet
Let
1\leq k<k^{\prime}<k^{\prime\prime}\leq r
,

8.3
The GQE Serre relations

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. Let
i\in I
, we denote by
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h}}={({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,p}})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
the solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{i},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
: see proposition
A.4
and theorem
A.6
. We recall that
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
designates the classical colouring of
\mathbb{B}
(see subsection
5.2
).

Definition-proposition 8.4
.

The following relations are called the GQE Serre relations associated to
\psi
, they are satisfied in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{l}\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}{(-1)}^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose k}\,\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{\pm}\big)^{k}\!\;X^{\pm}_{j}\!\;\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{\pm}\big)^{k^{\prime}}\ =\ 0\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,,\\
\text{where }\ {}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{\pm}\ :=\ \sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,\big(X_{i}^{\mp}\big)^{a}\!\;{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(\pm\check{\alpha}_{i})\!\;\big(X^{\pm}_{i}\big)^{a+1}\end{array}}\right.

Note that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}
(
i\in I
) converge in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
since the
sequences
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h}}
converge to zero for all
i\in I
.

Proof.

Fix
i\neq j
in
I
and let
V
be an integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. Let
a\in\mathbb{Z}
, we denote by
{U_{h,a}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
whose elements
x
satisfy
[\check{\alpha}_{i},x]=2ax
. Remark that
xy
belong
to
{U_{h,a+b}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
for every
x\in{U_{h,a}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
, for every
y\in{U_{h,b}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
and for
every
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
. Recall theorem
5.5
and denote by
\gamma
the
\Bbbk
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
defined by the following:

\displaystyle\gamma(X^{-})

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle X^{-}\,,

\displaystyle\gamma(H)\

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle H\,,

\displaystyle\gamma(X^{+})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}\,\big(X_{i}^{\mp}\big)^{a}\!\;{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,a}}(\pm\check{\alpha}_{i})\!\;\big(X^{\pm}_{i}\big)^{a+1}\,.

Since
\gamma(X^{+})
lies in
{U_{h,2}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
, the element
\gamma((X^{+})^{k})
belongs to
{U_{h,2k}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
for
every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the pull-back of
V
via
{\Delta^{i}_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\circ\gamma
is an integrable representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, we denote it by
V_{i}
. Thanks to proposition
1.1
, we know that the representation
V_{i}
is thus isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Regard the
\Bbbk
-vector space
\mathrm{End}_{\Bbbk}(V_{i})
as a representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
, where the actions
of
X^{\pm}
and
H
on a vector
f
are given by
[X^{\pm},f]
and
[H,f]
. Thanks to
proposition
1.1
again, on can identify the representation
\mathrm{End}_{\Bbbk}(V_{i})
with a product of
representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The element
X_{j}^{+}
, when viewed as a vector in
\mathrm{End}_{\Bbbk}(V_{i})
, lies in a subproduct of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(C_{ij})}
, since the following relations hold in the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
:

[\check{\alpha}_{i},X^{+}_{j}]\ =\ C_{ij}\;\!X^{+}_{j}\,,\quad\quad[X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{j}]\ =\ 0\,.

Therefore, the element
(X^{+})^{1-C_{ij}}
acts as zero on
X^{+}_{j}
, when viewed as a vector in
\mathrm{End}_{\Bbbk}(V_{i})
. This being true for every integrable representation
V

of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
, remark
6.6
implies that the following relation holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
:

\sum_{s+t=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{s}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose s}\,\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{+}\big)^{s}\;\!X^{+}_{j}\;\!\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{+}\big)^{t}\ =\ 0\,.

By considering the involutive
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra automorphism of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which sends
X^{\pm}_{i}
to
-X^{\mp}_{i}
and
\check{\mu}
to
-\check{\mu}
for every
i\in I
and
\check{\mu}\in Y
, one concludes.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

The GQE Serre relations associated to
\psi
are formal deformations of the classical Serre relations, i.e. the following equality holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}
:

\displaystyle\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}\Bigg(\,

\displaystyle\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{s}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose k}\,\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{\pm}\big)^{k}X^{\pm}_{j}\big({}^{\psi}\!X_{i}^{\pm}\big)^{k^{\prime}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose k}\,{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}\;\!X^{\pm}_{j}\;\!{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}\,,\quad\quad\forall\,i,j\in I,\ i\neq j\,.

9
The GQE conjecture and its consequences

9.1
The GQE conjecture

Conjecture

(GQE conjecture)
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring. Every representation in the category
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
admits a
\psi
-integrable
formal deformation
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
.

In the following of this section,
we assume that the statement of the GQE conjecture holds
. The author believes that the GQE conjecture is true, a proof of it is currently in preparation and will be published in a forthcoming paper. Note that the GQE algebras without Serre relations introduced in
section
8
will play a central role.

9.2
Formal deformations

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The
I
-colouring
\psi
is
h
-admissible.

(ii)

The
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-diagram
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proof.

The implication
ii)\Rightarrow i)
is a direct consequence of theorem
5.1
. Let us prove the converse. Suppose that assertion (i) holds.
Let
x_{h}
be an element in the ideal
I_{h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)

of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
and recall that we identify the specialisation at
{h\!=\!0}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
with
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
. Denote by
x_{0}
the image
in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
of
\pi_{|h=0}^{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}}(x_{h})
by
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. In view of the GQE
conjecture
Conjecture
, the element
x_{0}
acts as zero on all the representations of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. Hence, thanks to proposition
1.2
, the element
x_{0}
is zero. Since the functor
\bullet_{|h=0}
from the category of
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules to the category of
\Bbbk
-vector spaces is right-exact, we have proved that there exists a morphism
f
of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}

We thus have proved that there exists a morphism
g
of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}}
\scriptstyle{{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}}
\textstyle{{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}

Since the maps
{\pi_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}_{|h=0}
and
{\pi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
are surjective, the morphisms of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
g\circ f
and
f\circ g
are equal to identity maps. To sum up, we have proved that
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of the
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. One concludes thanks to theorem
5.1
.
∎

9.3
Presentation by generators and relations

In this subsection,
\psi
designates a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. Let
i\in I
, we denote by
{\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h}}={({\bar{S}^{\!\>\psi_{i}}_{\!\!\;h,p}})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
the solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{i},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
: see proposition
A.4
and theorem
A.6
. We recall that
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
designates the classical colouring of
\mathbb{B}
(see subsection
5.2
).

We denote by
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
topologically generated by
X^{\pm}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the non-deformable relations (
10
) of
\mathfrak{g}
and to the GQE Serre relations. The projection map from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
to
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
endows the latter with a structure of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra.

Theorem 9.2
.

The GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is isomorphic to the
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U^{\prime}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

Proof.

The proof is analog to the one given in rank 1 (see theorem
5.3
) and makes use of theorem
5.5
and proposition
8.4
.
∎

9.4
Y
-Triviality in finite type

We recall that
Y
designates also the
\Bbbk
-algebra
morphism from
\Bbbk[Y]
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
which sends
\check{\mu}
to
\check{\mu}
for every
\check{\mu}\in Y
.

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
. When the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is finite-dimensional, the GQE algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is a
Y\!
-trivial formal deformation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{g}
is finite-dimensional.
Thanks to theorem
9.1
, we know that
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is a formal deformation of the
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. Denote then by
\varphi_{h}
an isomorphism of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed

\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}[|h|]
. Fix
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
and denote by
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
the pull-back via
\varphi_{h}
of the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. Let now
v_{\lambda}
be a highest weight vector of
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
and let
i\in I
. In view of the relations of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
, the element
\check{\alpha}_{i}
in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
acts on
v_{\lambda}\in{V_{\!\!\;h}}
by a scalar in
\Bbbk[|h|]
. Since
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
is a
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
, the specialisation of this scalar to zero is
\langle\check{\alpha}_{i},\lambda\rangle
. Besides, we know that
{V_{\!\!\;h}}
is a weight representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. Therefore this scalar is
\lambda
. One concludes thanks to remark
6.6
.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

It was already known that the quantum group
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is a
{Y\!}
-trivial
deformation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
: see
[
7
]
.

9.5
Classification

Two
I
-colourings
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
are said congruent if the colourings
\psi_{i}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{i}
are for every
i\in I
.

Theorem 9.5
.

The following map is a bijection:

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$h$-admissible}\\
\text{$I$-colourings of $\mathbb{B}$,}\\
\text{up to congruence}\end{array}}\right\}

\displaystyle\ \longrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{Locally ${Y\!}$-trivial formal deformations}\\
\text{of the ${\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}$-pointed $\Bbbk$-diagram ${\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}$},\\
\text{up to isomorphism}\\
\text{of ${\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}$-pointed $\Bbbk[|h|]$-diagram}\end{array}}\right\}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\text{congruence}\\
\text{class of $\psi$}\end{array}

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\text{isomorphism}\\
\text{class of ${\mathbb{U}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}$.}\end{array}

Proof.

Consequence of theorems
5.6
and
9.1
.
∎

9.6
Representation theory

Let
\psi
be a
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
. We denote by
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the category of integrable representations of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which are topologically free when viewed as
\Bbbk[|h|]
-modules.

We denote by
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
whose objects
V
satisfy the following two conditions.

•

The weight spaces of
V
are of finite-rank over
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

•

There exist finitely many elements
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k}\in X
such that
\mathrm{wt}(V)
are contained in
\bigcup_{s=1}^{k}(\lambda_{s}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}\>\!\alpha_{i})
.

Let
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
, we denote by
{L_{h}(\lambda,\psi)}
the representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
topologically generated
by
v_{\lambda}
and subject to the following relations:

\check{\mu}\!\>.\!\>v_{\lambda}\ =\ \langle\lambda,\check{\mu}\rangle\!\;v_{\lambda}\,,\quad X^{+}_{i}.\!\>v_{\lambda}\ =\ 0\,,\quad(X^{-}_{i})^{\langle\lambda,\check{\alpha}_{i}\rangle+1}.\!\>v\ =\ 0\quad\ (i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
\psi
be a
h
-admissible
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
.

1)

A representation of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is in
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\;h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
if and only it is is
\psi
-integrable.

2)

For every
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
, the representation
{L_{h}(\lambda,\psi)}
is a formal deformation of
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

3)

The representations
{L_{h}(\lambda,\psi)}
(
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
) are pairwise distinct and are the unique, up to isomorphism, indecomposable representations in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

4)

Every representation in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is isomorphic to a topologically direct sum of representations
{L_{h}(\lambda,\psi)}
with
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
.

5)

Let
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
. Every
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(\lambda,\psi)}
to
{L_{h}(\lambda^{\prime},\psi)}
is equal to a scalar multiple of the identity map if
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
and is zero else.

6)

Every representation in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
admits a unique, up to isomorphism, formal deformation along
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

Proof.

The proof is analog to the one given in rank one (see theorem
5.4
) and makes use of proposition
3.5
.
∎

Appendix A
GQE equations

A.1
Notations

We denote by
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra formed by the lower triangular matrices of infinite order with coefficients in
{\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk[|h|]}}(\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{Z}})
(remark that the matrix multiplication for
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
is well defined since matrices in
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
are lower triangular):

\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])\ :=\ \left\{\mathbf{T}\in\left({\mathrm{End}_{\>\!\Bbbk[|h|]}}(\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{Z}})\right)^{\!\>\!\mathbb{N}^{2}};\,\ {\mathbf{T}_{\!\!\>p,a}}=0\,,\ \forall\,p,a\in\mathbb{N}\,\text{ s.t. }p<a\right\}\,.

We denote by
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module formed by the column matrices of infinite order with coefficients in
\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{Z}}
:

\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])\ :=\ \left(\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{Z}}\right)^{\!\!\;\mathbb{N}}\,.

We regard
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
as a left
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
-module, where the action of a matrix
\mathbf{T}

in
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
on a column matrix
M
in
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
is defined by the following:

{\big(\mathbf{T}\!\;.\!\;M\big)}_{\!\!\>p}\ :=\ \sum_{0\leq a\leq p}{\mathbf{T}_{\!\!\>p,a}}\!\;.\!\;M_{a}\quad\quad(p\in\mathbb{N})\,.

Let
M\in\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
and let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. We denote by
M[d]
the column matrix
in
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
defined by the following:

{\big(M[d]\big)}_{\!\!\;p}\ :=\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{ccl}M_{p-d}&&\text{if }p\geq d\,,\\
0&&\text{else}\end{array}}\right.\quad\quad\quad(p\in\mathbb{N})\,.

We denote by
\mathscr{E}_{h}
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module formed by the column matrices of infinite order with coefficients in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
converging to zero:

\mathscr{E}_{h}\ :=\ \left\{M\in\left(\Bbbk[u][|h|]\right)^{\!\!\;\mathbb{N}}\,;\,\ \lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}M_{p}=0\right\}\,.

We regard
\mathscr{E}_{h}
as a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-submodule of
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
.

A.2
Definition

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We denote by
\mathbf{T}(\psi)
the lower triangular matrix in
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
, defined by the following,
for
p,a\in\mathbb{N}
,
f\in\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\bigg[{\big(\mathbf{T}(\psi)\big)}_{\!\!\>p,a}\,.\,f\bigg](n)\ :=\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\displaystyle\frac{[\psi](n,p)!}{[\psi](n,p-a)!}\ f(n-2p+2a)&\quad\text{if }a\leq p\leq n\,,\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

We denote by
N(\psi)
and the column matrix in
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
, defined by the following,
for
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
:

{\big(N(\psi)\big)}_{\!\!\>p}\!\;(n)\ :=\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}[\psi](n,p)&\quad\ \text{if }0<p\leq n\,,\\
0&\quad\ \text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

Definition A.1
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. The GQE equation of degree
d
associated to
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, denoted by
{\mathscr{E}^{[d]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
, is the following linear equation:

\mathbf{T}(\psi_{1})\,.\,M\ =\ N(\psi_{2})[d]\,,

where the unknown
M
is a column matrix in
\mathscr{E}_{h}
.

A.3
First interpretation

Proposition A.2
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
M\in\mathscr{E}_{h}
. The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The equality
X^{+}X^{-}=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a}
holds in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

(ii)

The column matrix
M
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
.

Remark that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a}
converges in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
: the sequence
{(M_{p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to zero in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is separated and complete (we know from
lemma
4.10
that it is topologically free).

Proof.

Denote by
x
and
y
the elements
X^{+}X^{-}
and
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a}
in the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
. According to remark
4.9
, the equality
x=y
holds in the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
if and only if the actions of
x
and
y
are equal on all the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, assertion (i) is equivalent to the following one.

The equality
x.b_{n,p}=y.b_{n,p}
holds in
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\leq n
.

(i’)

Besides, for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\leq n
, the following equalities hold in the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
:

\displaystyle x.b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cr}\psi^{-}(n,p+1)\ \psi^{+}(n,n-p)\ b_{n,p}&\quad\text{if }p\neq n\,,\\
0&\quad\text{else}\,,\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle y.b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}M_{a}(n-2p+2a)\ \frac{\psi^{-}(n,p)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p-a)!}\ \frac{\psi^{+}(n,n-p+a)!}{\psi^{+}(n,n-p)!}\right]b_{n,p}\,.

As a consequence, assertion (i’) holds if and only if the column matrix
M
is a solution of the shifted GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
.
∎

A.4
Second interpretation

Proposition A.3
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be two invertible colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
and let
M\in\mathscr{E}_{h}
. The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

There exists a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
morphism
f
from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
such that the following holds.

\displaystyle\bullet\ \,

\displaystyle\left\{\!\!\!\>{\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(X^{-})&=&X^{-}\,,\\
f(H)&=&H\,,\\
f(X^{+})&=&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}\,.\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle\bullet\ \,

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{2})}
of
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
and the pull-back of
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
via
f
are isomorphic.

(ii)

The column matrix
M[1]
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
.

Remark that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}
converges in
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
: the sequence
{(M_{p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to zero in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
, the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-module
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
is separated and complete (we know from
lemma
4.10
that it is topologically free).

Proof.

Remark that the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
depends only on the congruence classes of the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
. The algebras
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
also do: see lemma
4.12
. We besides know that if
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
are two congruent colourings which divide each other in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
, then the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi)}
and
{L_{h}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
are isomorphic (see again lemma
4.12
). Therefore, and since the colourings
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
are invertible, one can without loss of generality assume that
\psi_{1}^{-}=\psi^{-}_{2}=1
. Denote by
g
the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
defined by the following:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lcl}g(X^{-})&:=&X^{-}\,,\\
g(H)&:=&H\,,\\
g(X^{+})&:=&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}\,.\end{array}}\right.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
g(X^{-})=X^{-}
and
g(H)=H
, every
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism between
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{2})}
and the pull-back of
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
via
g
is a scalar multiple of the identity map on
{\Bbbk\;\!B(n)[|h|]}
. Therefore and according to the definition of the algebra
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
, assertion (i) is equivalent to the following assertion.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the identity map on
{\Bbbk\;\!B(n)[|h|]}
is a
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{2})}
to the pull-back of
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
via
g
.

(i’)

Besides, for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0<p\leq n
, the following two equalities hold in the representations
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{1})}
and
{L_{h}(n,\psi_{2})}
, respectively:

\displaystyle g(X^{+}).\;\!b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq p-1}\,M_{a}(n-2p+2a+2)\ \frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-a-1)!}\ b_{n,p-1}\,,

\displaystyle X^{+}.\;\!b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\psi_{2}](n,p)\ b_{n,p-1}\,.

Therefore assertion (i’) holds if and only if the column matrix
M[1]
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
.
∎

A.5
Unicity of the solutions

Proposition A.4
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. There is at most one solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[d]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
.

Proof.

Let
M
be a column matrix in
(\Bbbk[u][|h|])^{\mathbb{N}}
which satisfies the following homogeneous linear equation:

\mathbf{T}(\psi_{1})\,.\,M\ =\ 0\,.

(37)

Let us prove by induction on
p\in\mathbb{N}
that
M_{p}
is zero. Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that
M_{p^{\prime}}
is zero for every
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<p}
. Fix now
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq p}
. In view of equation (
37
), the following equality holds in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}M_{a}(n-2p+2a)\ \frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-a)!}\ =\ 0\,.

Since
[\psi_{1}](n,p)!\neq 0
(recall that a colouring has by definition no zero) and thanks to the induction hypothesis, the specialisation of
M_{p}
at
n
is thus zero. This being true for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq p}
, the element
M_{p}\in\Bbbk[u][|h|]
is zero. The proposition follows.
∎

A.6
Classical solutions

We regard
(\mathscr{E}_{h})_{|h=0}
as a
\Bbbk
-vector subspace of
\mathscr{E}_{h}
. We denote by
{S^{\!\>[0]}_{\!\!\;\mathrm{cl}}}
and
{S^{\!\>[-1]}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
the column matrices in
(\mathscr{E}_{h})_{|h=0}
defined by the following:

\displaystyle{(S_{\mathrm{cl}})}_{0}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle u\,,\quad\quad

(S_cl)_1

:=

1   ,

(S_cl)_p

:=

0   (p ∈
N
_≥2)   ,

\displaystyle{(\bar{S}_{\mathrm{cl}})}_{0}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle 0\,,\quad\quad

(
¯

S

_cl)_1

:=

1   ,

(
¯

S

_cl)_p

:=

0   (p ∈
N
_≥2)   .

Recall the definition of the classical colouring
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
of
\mathbb{B}
:

(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ k\ \quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

Proposition A.5
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. Let
{S_{\!\!\;h}}
and
{\bar{S}_{\!\!\;h}}
be solutions of the GQE equations
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
and
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
, respectively. We assume that the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the deformation axiom.

1)

The column matrix
\pi_{|h=0}^{\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])}({S_{\!\!\;h}})
is equal to
S_{\mathrm{cl}}
.

2)

The column matrix
\pi_{|h=0}^{\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])}({\bar{S}_{\!\!\;h}})
