is equal to
\bar{S}_{\mathrm{cl}}
.

Proof.

The following equalities hold for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}{(S_{\mathrm{cl}})}_{a}(n-2p+2a)\ \frac{p\!\;!\,(n-p+1)!}{(p-a)!\,(n-p+a+1)!}\quad\quad

\displaystyle=\ (n-2p)\,+\,p\,(n-p+1)

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle(p+1)\,(n-p)\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}{(\bar{S}_{\mathrm{cl}})}_{a}(n-2p+2a)\ \frac{p\!\;!\,(n-p+1)!}{(p-a)!\,(n-p+a+1)!}

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle p\,(n-p+1)\,.

As a consequence, the column matrices
S_{\mathrm{cl}}
and
\bar{S}_{\mathrm{cl}}
are solutions of the GQE equations
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
, respectively. Since
\psi_{1}

and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the deformation axiom, the column matrices
\pi_{|h=0}^{\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])}({S_{\!\!\;h}})
and
\pi_{|h=0}^{\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])}({\bar{S}_{\!\!\;h}})
are also solutions of
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
and
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{\mathrm{cl}},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
, respectively. One concludes in view of the unicity of the solutions of GQE equations: see proposition
A.4
.
∎

A.7
Theorem

This subsection is dedicated to theorem
A.6
, whose statement is given below. We establish some preparatory lemmas in the next paragraphs, before giving a proof of the theorem in paragraph
A.7.8
.

A.7.1
Statement

Theorem A.6
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We assume that
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the deformation axiom.

1)

The GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{1})}
admits a solution if and only if
\psi_{1}
is
h
-admissible
.

2)

If
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are both
h
-admissible, then the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
admits a solution.

A.7.2
Lemma: shift of the solutions

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
M\in\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
. We assume that the colouring
\psi_{1}
is admissible. The following two assertions are equivalent.

1)

The column matrix
M
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
.

2)

The column matrix
M[1]
is a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{1}\psi_{2})}
.

Proof.

On the one hand, assertion (i) holds if and only if the following equality does for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\leq n
:

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}\,M_{a}(n-2p+2a)\,\!\>\frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-a)!}\ =\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{ccl}[\psi_{2}](n,p+1)&&\text{if }0<p+1\leq n\,,\\
0&&\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

On the other hand, assertion (ii) holds if and only if the following equality does for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\leq n
:

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}\,{\big(M[1]\big)}_{\!\!\>a}(n-2p+2a)\,\!\>\frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-a)!}\ =\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{ccl}[\psi_{1}\psi_{2}](n,p)&&\text{if }0<p\leq n\,,\\
0&&\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

One then concludes in view of the following equality, satisfied for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0<p\leq n
(recall that
\psi_{1}
is by assumption admissible):

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}\,{\big(M[1]\big)}_{\!\!\>a}(n-2p-2a)\,\!\>\frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-a)!}\\
=\ [\psi_{1}](n,p)\sum_{0\leq a\leq p-1}\,M_{a}(n-2(p-1)+2a)\,\!\>\frac{[\psi_{1}](n,p-1)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,p-1-a)!}\,\cdot

∎

A.7.3
Lemma: semi-regularity

Let
P_{h}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}P_{m}\,h^{m}
be an element in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
and let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. We denote
by
P_{h}(n)
the power series in
\Bbbk[|h|]
defined by the following:

P_{h}(n)\ :=\ \sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}P_{m}(n)\,h^{m}\,.

Let
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, we then regard the
\Bbbk[|h|]
-algebra
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
as a
\Bbbk[|h|]
-subalgebra

of
\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{N}_{\geq n^{\prime}}}
: an element
P_{h}\in\Bbbk[u][|h|]
is identified with the function in
\Bbbk[|h|]^{\mathbb{N}_{\geq n^{\prime}}}
whose value at
n
is
P(n)
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq n^{\prime}}
.

Definition A.8
.

A colouring
\psi
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
is said semi-regular if
[\psi](\,\cdot\,,k)\in\Bbbk[u][|h|]
, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. If the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution, then
\psi
is semi-regular.

Proof.

Suppose that the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution
S_{h}={(S_{h,p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let us prove by induction on
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
the following assertion.

[\psi](\,\cdot\,,k)\ \in\ \Bbbk[u][|h|]\,.

(
\ast_{k}
)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that assertion
(\ast_{k^{\prime}})
holds for every
k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq k}
. The
vector
S_{h}
being a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
, the following equality holds for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq k+1}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq k}S_{h,a}(n-2k+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](n,k)!}{[\psi_{1}](n,k-a)!}\ =\ [\psi](n,k+1)\,.

(38)

Assertion
(\ast_{k+1})
then holds by induction hypothesis.
∎

A.7.4
Lemma: quasi-regularity I

Definition A.10
.

A colouring
\psi
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[|h|]
is said quasi-regular if
\psi
is semi-regular and if the following condition holds:

\sup\big\{\deg_{u}\big({[\psi]}_{m}(u,k)\big)\,;\ k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}\big\}\ <\ \infty\,,\quad\quad\forall\,m\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Lemma A.11
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. If the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution, then
\psi
is quasi-regular.

Proof.

Suppose that the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution
S_{h}={(S_{h,p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
. We know from lemma
A.9
that the colouring
\psi
is semi-regular. Let us prove by induction on
m\in\mathbb{N}
the following assertion.

\sup\big\{\deg_{u}\big({[\psi]}_{m}(u,k)\big)\,;\ k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}\big\}\ <\ \infty\,.

(
\ast_{m}
)

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that assertion
(\ast_{m^{\prime}})
holds for every
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<m}
. The vector
S_{h}
being a solution of the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
, the following equalities hold in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq k}S_{h,a}(u-2k+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](u,k)!}{[\psi](u,k-a)!}\ =\ [\psi](u,k+1)\,,\quad\quad\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Besides, since the sequence
{(S_{h,k})}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to zero, there exists
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{h,k}\in h^{m+1}\Bbbk[u][|h|]
for all
k>k_{1}
. Therefore, the previous equalities imply the following ones in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]/h^{m+1}\Bbbk[u][|h|]
.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq k_{1}}S_{h,a}(u-2k+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](u,k)!}{[\psi](u,k-a)!}\ =\ [\psi](u,k+1)\,,\quad\quad\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq k_{1}}\,.

Assertion
(\ast_{m})
then holds by induction hypothesis.
∎

A.7.5
Lemma: quasi-regularity II

Lemma A.12
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We assume that the colourings
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
are quasi-regular and
that
\psi_{1}
satisfies the deformation axiom. Let
M
be a column matrix in
(\Bbbk[u][|h|])^{\mathbb{N}}
. If
{\mathbf{T}(\psi_{1})\!\>.\!\>M}=N(\psi_{2})
, then
M\in\mathscr{E}_{h}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
{\mathbf{T}(\psi_{1})\!\>.\!\>M}=N(\psi_{2})
. Let us prove by induction on
m\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
the following assertion.

There exists
p(m)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
M_{p}\in h^{m}\;\!\Bbbk[u][|h|]
for all
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq p(m)}
.

(
\ast_{m}
)

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
and suppose that assertion
(\ast_{m^{\prime}})
holds for every
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{<m}
. Thanks to the induction hypothesis, there exists
p(m-1)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
M_{p}
belongs to
{h^{m-1}\>\!\Bbbk[u][|h|]}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{>p(m-1)}
. For each
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote
by
M_{m,p}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
)
the elements in
\Bbbk[u]
such that
M_{p}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{M_{m,p}\>\!h^{m}}
. We besides denote by
D_{1}(m)
and
D_{2}(m)
the sets defined by the following.

\displaystyle D_{1}(m)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\deg_{u}\>\!(M_{p_{0},m_{0}})+\sum_{l=1}^{a}\deg_{u}\big({[\psi_{1}]}_{m_{l}}(u,p_{l})\big)\,;{\begin{array}[]{l}0\leq a\leq p(m-1)\,,\\
p_{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ \forall\,l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq a}\,,\\
\sum_{l=0}^{a}m_{l}=m-1\end{array}}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle D_{2}(m)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigg\{\deg_{u}\big({[\psi_{2}]}_{m-1}(u,p)\big)\,;\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}\bigg\}\,.

Let us prove by induction on
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
the following assertion.

\deg_{u}\>\!(M_{m-1,p})\,+\,p\ \leq\ \sup\big(D_{1}(m)\cup D_{2}(m)\big)\,.

(
\star_{p}
)

Remark first that
\deg_{u}\big({[\psi_{1}]}_{0}(u,p)\big)=1
for all
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, since by assumption the colouring
\psi_{1}
satisfies the deformation axiom. Fix
now
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
.
If
p\leq p(m-1)
, assertion
(\star_{p})
follows from the definition of
D_{1}(m)
. Suppose
that
p>p(m-1)
and that assertion
(\star_{p^{\prime}})
holds for
all
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{<p}
. Since
{\mathbf{T}(\psi_{1})\!\>.\!\>M}=N(\psi_{2})
, the following equality holds in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}M_{a}(u-2p+2a)\ \frac{[\psi_{1}](u,p)!}{[\psi_{1}](u,p-a)!}\ =\ [\psi_{2}](u,p+1)\,.

In view of the definition of
D_{1}(m),D_{2}(m)
, since besides
M_{a}\in{h^{m-1}\>\!\Bbbk[u][|h|]}
for every
a\in\mathbb{N}_{>p(m-1)}
, since
\deg_{u}\big({[\psi_{1}]}_{0}(u,p)\big)=1
for
all
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
and thanks to the induction hypothesis, assertion thus
(\star_{p})
holds. Besides, thanks to quasi-regularity of the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, we know that the sets
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are bounded. Assertion
(\star_{p})
being true for every
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
,
assertion
(\ast_{m})
then follows.
∎

A.7.6
Lemma: quotient axiom

Remark that the quotient axiom (see definition
4.13
of a
h
-admissible colouring) makes sense for a semi-regular colouring.

Lemma A.13
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. If the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution, then
\psi
is semi-regular and satisfies the quotient axiom.

Proof.

Suppose that the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
has a solution
S_{h}={(S_{h,p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
and let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We already know from lemma
A.9
that the colouring
\psi
is semi-regular. Therefore, the following equality holds for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\sum_{0\leq a\leq k}S_{h,a}(n-2k+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](n,k)!}{[\psi](n,k-a)!}\ =\ [\psi](n,k+1)\,.

Besides, since
S_{h}
is a solution of
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi)}
, the left-hand side of the previous equation is equal to zero when
n=k
. One concludes.
∎

A.7.7
Lemma: regularity

Recall the definition of the regularity axiom: see definition
4.13
of a
h
-admissible colouring.

Lemma A.14
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. If the colouring
\psi
is quasi-regular and satisfies the Verma axiom, then it satisfies the regularity axiom.

Proof.

Suppose that
\psi
is quasi-regular and satisfies the quotient axiom. Let us fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\psi
is quasi-regular, there exist
L\in\mathbb{N}
and
c_{l}\in\Bbbk^{\mathbb{N}^{\ast}}
(
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq L}
) such that the following holds:

{[\psi]}_{m}(n,k)\ =\ \sum_{l=0}^{L}\,c_{l}(k)\,n^{l}\,,\quad\quad\forall\,n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}.

Besides, since
\psi
satisfies the Verma axiom, the following equalities hold:

\sum_{l=0}^{L}\,c_{l}(k)\,(-n-2)^{l}\ =\ \sum_{l=0}^{L}\,c_{l}(n+k+1)\,n^{l}\,,\quad\quad\forall\,n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}.

As a consequence, the function
{\sum_{l=0}^{L}c_{l}(\,\cdot\,+n_{1})\!\>u^{l}}\in\Bbbk^{\mathbb{N}^{\ast}}
belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
, for every
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
. In order to establish the lemma, it is then sufficient to prove the following assertion

Let
{(f_{l})}_{0\leq l\leq L}\in(\Bbbk[u])^{L}
such that
\deg_{u}(f_{l})=l
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq L}
.
If
{\sum_{l=0}^{L}c_{l}(\,\cdot\,+n_{1})\!\>f_{l}}
belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
for every
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
, then so does
c_{l}
for every
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq L}
.

(
\ast_{L}
)

Let us prove assertion
(\ast_{L})
by induction on
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
L_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that assertion
(\ast_{L^{\prime}_{1}})
holds for every
L^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{<L_{1}}
. Let
{(f_{l})}_{0\leq l\leq L_{1}}\in(\Bbbk[u])^{L_{1}}
such that
\deg_{u}(f_{l})=l
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq L_{1}}
and suppose that
{\sum_{l=0}^{L_{1}}c_{l}(\,\cdot\,+n_{1})\!\>f_{l}}
belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
for every
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
. The following then holds for every
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
:

\sum_{l=0}^{L_{1}}\,c_{l}(\,\cdot\,+n_{1})\,\Big(f_{l}(u)-f_{l}(u-1)\Big)\ \in\ \Bbbk[u]\,.

Thanks to the induction hypothesis, the function
c_{l}
thus belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
for every
l\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq L_{1}}
. Since
{\sum_{l=0}^{L_{1}}c_{l}(\,\cdot\,+2)\!\>f_{l}}
belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
, the function
c_{0}
then also belongs to
\Bbbk[u]
. One concludes.
∎

A.7.8
Proof

We give here a proof of theorem
A.6
.

Proof.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathbf{T}\in\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
and let
M\in\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
, we denote by
\mathbf{T}_{|n}
and
M_{|n}
the matrices in
\Theta(\Bbbk[|h|])
and
\mathscr{E}(\Bbbk[|h|])
respectively, defined by the following:

\displaystyle{(\mathbf{T}_{|n})}_{p,a}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{\mathbf{T}_{\!\!\>p,a}}&\quad\text{if }a,p\leq n\,,\\
0&\quad\text{else}\end{array}}\right.

(p,a ∈
N
)   ,

\displaystyle{(M_{|n})}_{p}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\,M_{p}&\quad\ \text{if }p\leq n\,,\\
0&\quad\ \text{else}\end{array}}\right.

(p ∈
N
)   .

Let
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We denote by
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
the following equation:

{\mathbf{T}(\psi)}_{|n}\,.\,M_{|n}\ =\ {N(\psi^{\prime})}_{|n}\,,

where the unknown
M
is a column matrix in in
(\Bbbk[u][|h|])^{\mathbb{N}}
.

Claim 1
.

If
M
and
M^{\prime}
are two solutions of
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
, then
M_{|n}=M^{\prime}_{|n}
.

Proof.

We are going to adapt the proof of proposition
A.4
. Let
M
be a column matrix in
(\Bbbk[u][|h|])^{\mathbb{N}}
which satisfies the following homogeneous linear equation:

{\mathbf{T}(\psi)}_{|n}\,.\,M_{|n}\ =\ 0\,.

(42)

Let us prove by induction on
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
that
M_{p}
is zero. Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
and suppose that
M_{p^{\prime}}
is zero for every
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<p}
. Fix now
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq p}
. In view of
equation (
42
)
, the following equality holds in
\Bbbk[|h|]
.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq p}M_{a}(n^{\prime}-2p+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](n^{\prime},p)!}{[\psi](n^{\prime},p-a)!}\ =\ 0\,.

Since
[\psi](n^{\prime},p)!\neq 0
(recall that a colouring has by definition no zero) and thanks to the induction hypothesis, the specialisation of
M_{p}
at
n^{\prime}
is thus zero. This being true for every
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq p}
, the element
M_{p}\in\Bbbk[u][|h|]
is zero. The claim follows.
∎

Suppose now that the colourings
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are semi-regular and satisfy the quotient axiom. Consider then the following two assertions.

\displaystyle{[}\psi{]}(l,n+1)\ =\ {[}\psi{]}(-l-2,n-l)\,,\quad\ \forall\,l\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\,.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad(\text{i${}_{n}$)}

The equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
has a solution.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad(\text{ii${}_{n}$)}

Claim 2
.

We assume that
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are semi-regular and satisfy the quotient axiom. We moreover assume that
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom.

1)

Assume that
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
satisfy the Verma axiom. If assertion (ii
{{}_{\!\;n}}
) holds, then assertion (ii
{{}_{\!\;{n+1}}}
) holds.

2)

Assume that
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are equal. If assertions (i
{{}_{\!\;n}}
) and (i
{{}_{\!\;n+1}}
) hold, then assertions (i
{{}_{\!\;{n+1}}}
)
and (ii
{{}_{\!\;{n+1}}}
)
are equivalent.

Proof.

Let
M
be a solution of
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
. Assertion (ii
n+1
) then holds if and only if there exists
x\in\Bbbk[u][|h|]
such that the following equality holds in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
(recall that
\psi^{\prime}
satisfies the quotient axiom):

\sum_{a=0}^{n}\,M_{a}(u-2n-2+2a)\,\frac{[\psi](u,n+1)!}{[\psi](u,n+1-a)!}\,+\,x\,\big([\psi](u,n+1)!\big)\ =\ [\psi^{\prime}](u,n+2)\,.

Besides, since
\psi
satisfies the deformation axiom, there exists an invertible element
y
in
\Bbbk[u][|h|]
such that
[\psi](u,n+1)!={y\>\!\prod_{l=0}^{n}(u-l)}
. Therefore, assertion (ii
n+1
) holds if and only if the following equalities do.

\sum_{0\leq a\leq n}M_{a}(l-2n-2+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](l,n+1)!}{[\psi](l,n+1-a)!}\ =\ [\psi^{\prime}](l,n+2)\,,\quad\quad\forall\,l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}\,.

Suppose now that assertion (i
{{}_{\!\;n}}
) holds (remark that assertion (i
{{}_{\!\;n}}
) does if
\psi
satisfies the Verma axiom). Since besides
M
is a solution of
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
, the following equalities then hold for every
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
(recall also that
\psi_{1}
satisfies the quotient axiom):

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq n}M_{a}(l-2n-2+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](l,n+1)!}{[\psi](l,n+1-a)!}

\displaystyle=\ \sum_{0\leq a\leq n-l}M_{a}(l-2n-2+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](l,n+1)!}{[\psi](l,n+1-a)!}

\displaystyle[\text{quotient axiom}]

\displaystyle=\ \sum_{0\leq a\leq n-l}M_{a}(-l-2-2(n-l)+2a)\ \frac{[\psi](-l-2,n-l)!}{[\psi](l,n-l-a)!}

\displaystyle[\text{assertion (\text{i${{}_{\!\;n}}$})}]

\displaystyle=\ [\psi^{\prime}](-l-2,n-l+1)\,.

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\![\text{$M$ solution of ${\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}$}]

Therefore, assertion assertion (ii
n+1
) holds if and only if the following equality does for every
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

[\psi^{\prime}](l,n+2)\ =\ [\psi^{\prime}](-l-2,n-l+1)\,.

One concludes.
∎

Claim 3
.

Assume that
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are quasi-regular, satisfy the quotient axiom and the Verma axiom. The GQE equations
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
and
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi,{\psi\!\;\psi^{\prime}})}
admit a solution.

Proof.

Remark that assertion (ii
0
) straightforwardly holds. Claim
2
then implies by induction that the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h,n}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
admits a solution
{(M_{n,p})}_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Denote by
S_{h}
the column matrix in
(\Bbbk[u][|h|])^{\mathbb{N}}
defined by the following:
S_{h,p}:={(M_{p,p})}_{p}
(
p\in\mathbb{N}
). Claim
1
implies that
{\mathbf{T}(\psi)\!\>.\!\>S_{h}=N(\psi^{\prime})}
. Since
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are quasi-regular, the column matrix
S_{h}
then converges to zero: see lemma
A.12
. Therefore, the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi,\psi^{\prime})}
has a solution and so does the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi,{\psi\!\;\psi^{\prime}})}
thanks to lemma
A.7
.
∎

Let us prove point 1 of the theorem. Suppose that
\psi_{1}
is
h
-admissible. Claim
3
then implies that the GQE equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{1})}
admits a solution. Conversely, suppose that
{\mathscr{E}^{[-1]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{1})}
admits a solution. We then know from lemmas
A.11
and
A.13
that
\psi_{1}
satisfies the quotient axiom and is quasi-regular. Remark besides that assertions (i
0
) and (ii
0
) straightforwardly hold. Claim
2
then implies by induction that
\psi_{1}
satisfies the Verma axiom. Since
\psi_{1}
satisfies by assumption the deformation axiom, it is then
h
-admissible. Let us now prove point 2 of the theorem. Suppose that the colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are
h
-admissible. Since the equation
{\mathscr{E}^{[0]}_{h}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}
depends only on the congruence classes of
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, one can assume without loss of generality that
\psi^{-}_{1}=1
. Since besides
\psi_{1}
satisfies the deformation axiom, it is then invertible in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{h}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. One concludes from claim
3
with
\psi=\psi_{1}
and
\psi^{\prime}=\psi_{2}/\psi_{1}
.
∎

partie II
Coloured Kac-Moody Algebras, Isogenic and Langlands Interpolations

Notations and conventions

Rings and associative algebras are unitary, their morphisms are unity-preserving.

We denote by
\Bbbk
a field of
characteristic zero
. We denote by
R
a (commutative)
\Bbbk
-algebra which is an
integral domain
when viewed as a ring. We designate by
K(R)
its field of fractions. When considering topological
R
-algebras, we
regard
R
as a topological ring, endowed with the discrete topology.

We denote by
\Bbbk[u]
the polynomial
\Bbbk
-algebra in one variable
u
and we denote
by
\Bbbk[u^{\pm 1}]
the Laurent polynomial
\Bbbk
-algebra.

Let
\Lambda
be a set. We denote by
\Bbbk\Lambda
the
\Bbbk
-vector space which admits
\Lambda
as a basis. We denote by
\langle\Lambda\rangle
the free monoid generated by
\Lambda
. We denote by
\Bbbk\langle\Lambda\rangle
the
\Bbbk
-algebra of this monoid. We denote by
{\Lambda^{\!R}}
the ring of functions from
R
to
\Lambda
.

Let
A
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra and let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra. Left
A
-modules and left
\mathfrak{g}
-modules are called representations of
A
and
\mathfrak{g}
, respectively. Morphisms of left
A
-modules and of left
\mathfrak{g}
-modules are called
A
-morphisms and
\mathfrak{g}
-morphisms, respectively.

We identify as usual representations of a Lie algebra with representations of its universal enveloping algebra. The universal enveloping algebra of the Lie
R
-algebra
{\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\!R}
is denoted by
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

A sum indexed by a empty set is zero, a product indexed by an empty set is equal to
1
.

1
Kac-Moody algebras

We recall here the definition of Kac-Moody algebras. Note that the definition given in this paper is slightly different (but mainly equivalent) to the definition of a Kac-Moody algebra usually present in the literature. The difference is about the Cartan subalgebra, which is built here from what we call a root datum (we borrow the name and the concept from Lusztig: see
[
35
]
). We also recall some results on the representation theory of Kac-Moody algebras.

1.1
Generalised Cartan matrices and root data

A
generalised Cartan matrix
is a matrix
C={(C_{ij})}_{i,j\in I}
indexed by a finite
set
I
, with entries in
\mathbb{Z}
and which satisfies the following conditions for all
i,j\in I
.

•

C_{ii}=2
.

•

C_{ij}\leq 0
if
i\neq j
.

•

C_{ij}=0
if and only if
C_{ji}=0
.

The set
I
is called the
Dynkin set
. The elements of
I
are called the
Dynkin vertices
. The generalised Cartan matrix
C
is called
symmetrisable
if there exists a family
{(d_{i})}_{i\in I}
, called a
symmetrising vector
of
C
, with values in
\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and such that
d_{i}\>\!C_{ij}=d_{j}\>\!C_{ji}
for
all
i,j

in
I
. We assume that the integers
d_{i}
(
i\in I^{\prime}
) are coprime for every connected component
I^{\prime}
of
I
(two elements
i,j\in I
are linked if
C_{ij}\neq 0
). In this paper,
we always assume that a generalised Cartan matrix is symmetrisable
.

A generalised Cartan matrix is of
finite type
if it is non-degenerate (or positive-definite, equivalently). Generalised Cartan matrices of finite type are also called Cartan matrices.

A
root datum

\mathfrak{X}
associated to a generalised Cartan matrix
C
consists of the following.

•

A pair
(X,Y)
where
X
,
Y
are two finitely generated free
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

•

Families
(\alpha_{i}\>\!;\,i\in I)
and
{({\check{\alpha}_{i}}\>\!;\,i\in I)}
with values in
X
and
Y
, respectively. We demand that the maps
i\mapsto\alpha_{i}
and
i\mapsto\check{\alpha}_{i}
are injective.

•

A perfect bilinear pairing
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle:Y\times X\to\mathbb{Z}
such that
\langle{\check{\alpha}_{i}},\alpha_{j}\rangle=C_{ij}
for all
i,j\in I
.

The
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
X
and
Y
are called the
root lattice
and the
coroot lattice
, respectively. We call
\alpha_{i}
and
{\check{\alpha}_{i}}
(
i\in I
) the
simple roots
and the
simple coroots
, respectively. Where there is no possibility for confusion, we normally abuse notation slightly and write
(X,Y,I)
to designate the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
. The root datum is said of
finite type
if the generalised Cartan matrix
C
is of finite type.

We say that the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is
X\!
-regular
if the family of simple roots is linearly independent and
Y\!
-regular
if the family of simple coroots is. When
\mathfrak{X}
is of finite type,
X\!
-regularity and
Y\!
-regularity are automatic by the non-degeneracy of
C
, but in general it is an additional assumption, which we make in this paper.

An element
\lambda
in
X
is said
dominant
if
\langle{\check{\alpha}_{i}},\lambda\rangle\geq 0
for all
i\in I
. The subset of dominant elements in
X
is denoted by
{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
and called the
dominant cone
.

On the root lattice
X
, we define a partial order (remark that the antisymmetry axiom is satisfied thanks to the
X\!
-regularity):

\lambda\ \leq\ \lambda^{\prime}\quad\ \text{ if }\ \quad\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda\ \in\ \sum_{i\in I}\,\mathbb{N}\>\!\alpha_{i}\,.

1.2
Kac-Moody algebras with and without Serre relations

The
Kac-Moody algebra

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{X})
associated to the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
and subject to the following relations:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rclr}\big[\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\big]&=&0&(\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[{\check{\mu}},X^{\pm}_{i}\big]&=&\pm\;\!\langle{\check{\mu}},\alpha_{i}\rangle\,X_{i}^{\pm}&(i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{j}\big]&=&0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,,\end{array}}\right.

(43)

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lr}\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]=\check{\alpha}_{i}&(i\in I)\,,\\
\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\choose k}\,{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}\;\!X^{\pm}_{j}\;\!{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}=0&\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

(44)

The universal enveloping algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
of
\mathfrak{g}
is the
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i}
,
X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the relations (
43
) and (
44
).

The relations
\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}{1-C_{ij}\choose k}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}X^{\pm}_{j}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}=0
(
i,j\in I
,
i\neq j
) are called the
Serre relations
. The elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
) are called the
Chevalley generators
of
\mathfrak{g}
.

We denote by
{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U^{>0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the
\Bbbk
-subalgebras of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i}
(
i\in I
) and
X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), respectively. We denote by
{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the
\Bbbk
-subalgebra
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
generated by
Y
. The algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
, when viewed as a
\Bbbk
-vector space, admits the following decomposition, where the isomorphism is given by the multiplication of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
:

{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\ \simeq\ \,{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\otimes_{\Bbbk}\,{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\otimes_{\Bbbk}\,{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}\,.

We call this decomposition the
triangular decomposition
of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

The Kac-Moody algebra without Serre relations

\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{X})
associated to the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the relations (
43
) and to the following ones:

\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]=\check{\alpha}_{i}\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

(45)

The universal enveloping algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
of
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}
is the
\Bbbk
-algebra
generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i}
,
X^{+}_{i}
(
i\in I
), the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, subject to the relations (
43
)
and (
45
)
. We denote by
\varpi_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})
the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}

to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. The algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
also admits a triangular decomposition.

1.3
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
and the simple
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-directions

We designate by
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
the root datum defined by the following.

•

The root and coroot lattices are both equal to
\mathbb{Z}
.

•

The simple root and the simple coroot are
2\in\mathbb{Z}
and
1\in\mathbb{Z}
, respectively.

•

The pairing is the product of
\mathbb{Z}
.

Note that the Kac-Moody algebra associated to
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
is naturally isomorphic to the Lie algebra of 2-by-2 matrices with coefficients in
\Bbbk
and whose trace is zero; we denote it by
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

The two Chevalley generators of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
are designated by
X^{-}
and
X^{+}
, we besides denote by
H
the simple coroot of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
. The Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is generated by the elements
X^{-},X^{+}
,
H
and subject to the following relations:

\displaystyle\big[H,X^{\pm}\big]

\displaystyle\ =\

\displaystyle\pm\;\!2\,X^{\pm}\,,

(46)

\displaystyle\big[X^{+},X^{-}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H\,.

(47)

Remark that there is no Serre relations for
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

Let
i\in I
. There exist unique Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra morphisms from
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
to
\mathfrak{g}

and
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}
, which
sends
X^{\pm}
and
H
to
X_{i}^{\pm}
and
\check{\alpha}_{i}
, respectively. The induced
\Bbbk
-algebra morphisms from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
are denoted by
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
, respectively. The morphisms
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
(
i\in I
) are called the
simple
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-directions

of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})}
, respectively.

1.4
Highest weight representations and the category
\mathcal{O}

Let
V
be a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
and let
\lambda\in X
. A vector
v
in
V
is called a
weight vector
of weight
\lambda
if
\check{\mu}.v=\langle\check{\mu},\lambda\rangle\;\!v
for all
\check{\mu}\in Y
. The
weight space

of
V
of
weight
\lambda
is the
\Bbbk
-vector subspace of
V
whose vectors are of weight
\lambda
, we denote it
by
V_{\lambda}
. Remark that the sum of the weight spaces of
V
is direct. The representation
V
is called a
weight representation

of
\mathfrak{g}

if
V
is equal to the sum of its weights spaces. If
V
is a weight representation and if
V_{\lambda}
is nonzero, we call
\lambda
a
weight
of
V
. The set of weights of a weight
representation
V
is denoted
by
\mathrm{wt}(V)
.

A representation
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
is called a
highest weight representation
of
\mathfrak{g}
of highest weight
\lambda
if the following two conditions hold.

•

The representation
V
is generated by a nonzero vector
v_{\lambda}
in
V_{\lambda}
. The vector
v_{\lambda}
is called a
highest weight vector
of
V
.

•

The weights of
V
are less than or equal to
\lambda
.

Remark that a highest weight representation of
\mathfrak{g}
is a weight representation
of
\mathfrak{g}
with finite-dimensional weight spaces.

We denote by
{\mathcal{O}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the category of representations
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
which satisfy the following conditions.

•

The representation
V
is a weight representation of
\mathfrak{g}
with finite-dimensional weight spaces.

•

There exist finitely many elements
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k}
in
X
such that
\mathrm{wt}(V)
is contained in
\bigcup_{s=1}^{k}(\lambda_{s}-\sum_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}\alpha_{i})
.

1.5
Some known results

A weight representation
V
of
\mathfrak{g}
is called
integrable
if the actions of the Chevalley generators on
V
are locally nilpotent. We denote by
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
the category of integrable representations of
\mathfrak{g}
. When the root datum
\mathfrak{X}
is of finite type (equivalently, when
\mathfrak{g}
is finite-dimensional), a representation of
\mathfrak{g}
is in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
if and only if it is finite-dimensional.

Let us recall some results on the representation theory of
\mathfrak{g}
.

•

For every
\lambda\in X
, there is a unique, up to isomorphism, irreducible representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of
\mathfrak{g}
of highest weight
\lambda
.

•

Let
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
. The representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
is generated by a vector
v_{\lambda}
and subject to the following relations:

\check{\mu}.v_{\lambda}\,=\,\langle\lambda,\check{\mu}\rangle\;\!v_{\lambda}\,,\quad X^{+}_{i}.v_{\lambda}=0\,,\quad(X^{-}_{i})^{\langle\lambda,\check{\alpha}_{i}\rangle+1}.v_{\lambda}\,=\,0\quad\ (i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,.

•

The representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
(
\lambda\in X
) are pairwise distinct and are the unique, up to isomorphism, irreducible representations in
{\mathcal{O}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

•

Let
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in X
. Every
\mathfrak{g}
-morphism from
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
to
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda^{\prime})}
is equal to a scalar multiple of the identity map if
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
and is zero else.

•

Let
\lambda\in X
. The representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
is integrable if and only if
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
.

•

Every representation in the category
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
with
\lambda\in{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
.

We give below, for the reader’s convenience, a proof of a complete-reducibility type result for the category of integrable representations of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.

Proposition 1.1
.

An integrable representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Besides, a representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
is integrable if and only if the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
are locally finite.

Proof.

Let
V
be a representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
on which the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
are locally finite and let
v
be a vector in
V
. Denote by
V^{\prime}
the subrepresentation of
V
generated by
v
. In view of the triangular decomposition of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
and since the actions of
X^{-},X^{+},H
on
V
are locally finite, the subrepresentation
V^{\prime}
is finite-dimensional. Therefore,
V^{\prime}
is a sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. This being true for
every
v
in
V
, the representation
V
is a sum of irreducible representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the representation
V
is a direct sum of irreducible representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

The author ignores whether there exists (when
\mathfrak{g}
is not finite-dimensional) a reference for the following proposition. The proof given below follows from a private communication with Victor Kac. Note that it is possible in the case
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
to give a more direct and elementary proof.

Proposition 1.2
.

An element in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the representations of
\mathfrak{g}
in
{\mathcal{O}^{\!\>\mathrm{int}}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

Proof.

Consider the
{X\!}
-gradation of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
defined by the following:

\mathrm{deg}\;\!(\mu)\,:=\,0\,,\quad\quad\mathrm{deg}\;\!(X^{\pm}_{i})\,:=\,\alpha_{i}\quad\quad\quad(\mu\in X,\ i\in I)\,.

Let
x
be a nonzero element in
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
. Recall the triangular decomposition of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and pick nonzero homogeneous
elements
x^{-}_{k}
,
x^{0}_{k}

and
x^{+}_{k}

in
{U^{<0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
,
{U^{0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
and
{U^{>0}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
respectively
(
1\leq k\leq l
)
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

a)

x=\sum_{1\leq k\leq l}x^{-}_{k}\;\!x^{0}_{k}\;\!x_{k}^{+}
,

b)

the family
{(x^{-}_{k})}_{1\leq k\leq l}
is
\Bbbk
-linearly independent,

c)

\deg(x_{1}^{+})\ngtr\deg(x_{k}^{+})
for every
1<k\leq l
.

Denote by
\tau
the involutive anti-automorphism of the
\Bbbk
-algebra
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
which sends
X_{i}^{\pm}

to
-X_{i}^{\mp}
for every
i\in I
and which fixes pointwise
Y
. Pick
\lambda
in
{X_{\!\!\>\mathrm{dom}}}
such that the following conditions hold (we denote by
v_{\lambda}
a fixed choice of highest weight vector of the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
):

a’)

x^{0}_{1}(\lambda)
is nonzero,

b’)

the family
{(x^{-}_{k}.v_{\lambda})}_{1\leq k\leq l}
is
\Bbbk
-linearly independent,

c’)

the vector
\tau(x^{+}_{1}).v_{\lambda}
is nonzero.

Pick now a nonzero
\Bbbk
-bilinear form
\sigma
on
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
which has the following properties:

a”)

\sigma
is non-degenerate,

b”)

\sigma(v,w)=0
for every weight vectors
v,w
in
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of distinct weights,

c”)

\sigma
is contravariant, i.e.
\sigma(y.v,w)=\sigma(v,\tau(y).w)
for every
v,w\in{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
and for every
y\in{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

In view of the non-degeneracy of
\sigma
and in view of the orthogonality property
b^{\prime\prime})
, pick a weight vector
v
in
{L_{\Bbbk}(\lambda)}
of weight
\lambda-\deg(x_{1}^{+})
such that
\sigma(v,\tau(x^{+}_{1}).v_{\lambda})
is nonzero. Since
\sigma
is contravariant, the vector
x_{1}^{+}.v
is thus a nonzero scalar multiple of
v_{\lambda}
. Thanks to condition
c)
above, the following holds:

x.v\ =\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq l,\\
\deg(x^{+}_{k})\,=\,\deg(x_{1}^{+})\end{subarray}}x^{0}_{k}(\lambda)\,x^{-}_{k}.(x^{+}_{k}.v)\,.

In view of conditions
a^{\prime})
and
b^{\prime})
, one concludes that
x.v
is nonzero.
∎

2
Coloured Kac-Moody algebras

2.1
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed algebras

Recall the definition of the Kac-Moody algebra
\mathfrak{g}
: see subsection
1.2
. The
relations (
43
)
are called the
non-deformable relations

of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Definition 2.1
.

We denote by
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
the Lie
\Bbbk
-algebra generated by
X^{\pm}_{i}
(
i\in I
)
, the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
Y
, and subject to the non-deformable relations of
\mathfrak{g}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rclr}\big[\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\big]&=&0&(\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[{\check{\mu}},X^{\pm}_{i}\big]&=&\pm\;\!\langle{\check{\mu}},\alpha_{i}\rangle\,X_{i}^{\pm}&(i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{j}\big]&=&0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

When
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
, we denote
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
by
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
.

Let
V
be a representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
and let
\lambda\in X
. A vector
v
in
V
is called a
vector of weight

\lambda
if
\check{\mu}.v={\langle\check{\mu}\,,\lambda\rangle\!\>v}
for all
\check{\mu}\in Y
. The
weight space

of
V
of
weight
\lambda
is the
R
-submodule
of vectors of
weight
\lambda
in
V
, we denote it
by
V_{\lambda}
. We call
\lambda
a
weight
of
V

if
V_{\lambda}
is nonzero, the set of weights of
V
is denoted
by
\mathrm{wt}(V)
. Remark that the sum of weight spaces of
V
is direct. The representation
V
is called a
weight representation
if
V
is equal to the sum of its weights spaces.

Definition 2.2
.

If
V
is a weight representation and if
V
is free when viewed as a
R
-module, we say that
V
admits a character and we denote by
\chi(V)
the function from
X
to
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
which associates to
\lambda\in X
the rank of the free
R
-module
V_{\lambda}
.

A representation
V
of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is said
integrable
if it is a weight representation and if the action of
X^{\pm}_{i}

on
V
is locally nilpotent for every
i\in I
.

Definition 2.3
.

A topological
R
-algebra
A
is said
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed if
A
is endowed with a
R
-algebra morphism
\dot{A}
from
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
to
A
, called the
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-point of
A
, and whose image is dense in
A
.

Every element in
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is regarded as an element in
A
as well and every representation
V
of
A
is viewed also as a representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, thanks to the pull-back of
V
via the
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-point
\dot{A}
. The notions of weight representations, characters and integrability are then accordingly defined for representations of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed algebras.

2.2
The global crystal of rank 1

Definition 2.4
.

The global crystal of rank 1, denoted by
\mathbb{B}
, is the quiver defined by the following.

•

The set
B
of vertices of
\mathbb{B}
is
{\big\{b_{n,p}:=(n,n-2p)\;\!;\,n,i\in\mathbb{N},\,p\leq n\big\}}
.

•

The set
E
of edges of
\mathbb{B}
is the disjoint union
{E^{-}\sqcup E^{+}}
of two copies
of
[E]:={\big\{(n,k)\;\!;\,n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\,k\leq n\big\}}
. The source of the edge
(n,k)\in E^{\pm}
is
(n,\pm(n-2k+2))
and its target is
(n,\pm(n-2k))
.

Here is an illustration of the global crystal of rank 1. Remark that there is no arrow for
n=0
.

\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \iddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\iddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{1,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{0,0}}}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \cdots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{1,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{2,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{3,3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\ \ \ddots}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote by
\mathbb{B}_{n}
the connected component of
\mathbb{B}
containing the vertex
b_{n,0}
. We denote by
B_{n}
and
E_{n}
the sets of vertices and edges in
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, respectively. The quiver
\mathbb{B}_{n}
is called the
n
th component of
\mathbb{B}
.

Let
f\in R^{[E]}
and let
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k\leq n
. We denote by
f(n,k)!
the element in
R
defined by the following:

f(n,k)!\ :=\ \left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\prod_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}f(n,k^{\prime})&\quad\ \text{if }k\geq 1\,,\\
0&\quad\ \text{else.}\end{array}}\right.

2.3
Colourings

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, remark that the irreducible representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
can be defined as the
\Bbbk
-vector space
{\Bbbk B_{n}}
, where the actions of
X^{\pm},H
are given by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,\\
&&\\
X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}(p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.\\
&&\\
X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}(n-p+1)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}\right.

Definition 2.5
.

•

We denote by
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
the ring
R^{E}
, an element is called a colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
.

•

We denote by
{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
the ring
({\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})})^{I}
, an element
\psi={(\psi_{i})}_{i\in I}
is called an
{I\!\!\>}
-colouring of the crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
.

When the cardinality of
I
is
1
, we identify the rings
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and
{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and we identify colourings with
I
-colourings.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We denote by
\psi^{\pm}
the restriction of
\psi
to
E^{\pm}
. Here is an illustration of the colouring
\psi
over the
n
th component of
\mathbb{B}
.

\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,1)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,n)}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,2)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,n-1)}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,n-1)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\ \,\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,2)}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{-}(n,n)}
\textstyle{\quad\ \,\!\;\bullet\!\;\scriptstyle{b_{n,n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\scriptstyle\psi^{+}(n,1)}

Definition 2.6
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote
by
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
the representation
of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
whose underlying
R
-module
is
{RB_{n}}
and where the actions of
X^{\pm},H
are defined by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,\\
&&\\
X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{-}(n,p+1)\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.\\
&&\\
X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}\psi^{+}(n,n-p+1)\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}\right.

Remark at once that the representation
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
is a
R
-free integrable representation of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The representation
{L_{\!\!\>K(R)}(n,\psi)}
is irreducible if and only if the restriction of the function to
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero.

Proof.

Suppose that the restriction of the function
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero and let
V
be a subrepresentation of
{L_{\!\!\>K(R)}(n,\psi)}
. Since
{L_{\!\!\>K(R)}(n,\psi)}
is a weight representation of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
, so is
V
. Hence,
V
is nonzero if and only if there exists
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
such that
b_{n,p}\in V
. Besides, in view of the action of
X^{\pm}
on the representation
{L_{\!\!\>K(R)}(n,\psi)}
, remark that the subrepresentation of
{L_{\!\!\>K(R)}(n,\psi)}
generated by the vector
b_{n,p}
is proper if and only if the restriction of
\psi
to
E_{n}
has no zero. One concludes.
∎

Definition 2.8
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

•

The
I
-colouring
\psi
is said non-degenerate if
\psi_{i}
has no zero for every
i\in I
.

•

The
I
-colouring
\psi
is said admissible (over
R
) if it is invertible in
{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

Remark that an admissible
I
-colouring is in particular non-degenerate.

2.4
Tannakian topologies

Let
A
be
R
-algebra, let
\mathcal{C}
be a class of representations of
A
and let
B
be a topological
R
-algebra
.

Definition 2.9
.

•

The Tannakian topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
generated by the class
\mathcal{C}
is the coarsest topology on
A
which makes continuous every map
{A\!\times\!V\!\to\!V}
associated to a representation
V
in
\mathcal{C}
, where
V
is endowed with the discrete topology.

•

A representation
V
of
B
is said
discrete
if the associated map
{B\!\times\!V\!\to\!V}
is continuous, where
V
is endowed with the discrete topology.

Remark that the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
is an initial topology: more precisely, it is the coarsest topology on
A
which makes continuous every map
{A\!=\!A\!\times\!\{v\}\!\to\!V}
associated to a representation
V\in\mathcal{C}
and a vector
v\in V
, where
V
is endowed with the discrete topology.

Proposition 2.10
.

1)

The
R
-algebra
A
endowed with the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
is a topological
R
-algebra.

2)

The following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The topological
R
-algebra
A
is separated.

(ii)

An element in
A
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the representations in
\mathcal{C}
.

3)

A sequence
{(x_{n})}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
A
converges to zero if and only if the sequence
{(x_{n}.v)}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is eventually zero, for every
V\in\mathcal{C}
and
v\in V
.

Proof.

Let
V\in\mathcal{C}
, let
v\in V
and denote by
I(v)
the left ideal of
A
formed by the elements acting by zero on the vector
v
. Let
x\in A
and remark that
I(v)
contains
{I(x.v)\!\>x}
. As a consequence, the
R
-submodules
I(v)
of
A
, with
v\in V
and
V\in\mathcal{C}
, form a local basis at the origin of a
R
-linear topology which endows the
R
-algebra
A
with a structure of topological
R
-algebra. This topology is the Tannakian topology generated by
\mathcal{C}
.
Points 1
and 2 of the proposition then follow. Point 3 is a consequence of the initial form of the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
.
∎

We regard from now
A
as a topological
R
-algebra, endowed with the Tannakian topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
. Remark that a representation in
\mathcal{C}
is a discrete representation of
A
.

Proposition 2.11
.

Let
f
be a
R
-algebra morphism from
B
to
A
.

1)

The map
f
is continuous if and only if the pull-back of every representation in
\mathcal{C}
via
f
is a discrete representation of
B
.

2)

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
be a class of representations of
B
and suppose that
B
is endowed with the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
. If the pull-back via
f
of every representation
in
\mathcal{C}
is in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, then the map
f
is continuous.

Proof.

Remark that a representation
V
of
B
is continuous if and only if every map
{B\!\times\!\{v\}\!\to\!V}
associated to the representation
V
and to a vector
v\in V
, is continuous, where
V
is endowed with the discrete topology. Point 1 is then a consequence of the initial form of the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C})
. Point 2 follows from
point 1
and from the definition of the topology
\tau(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
∎

Denote by
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
the (separated) completion of
A
. By definition,
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
is a topological
R
-algebra, which comes with a continuous
R
-algebra morphism
\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{C})
from
A
to
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
.

Proposition 2.12
.

For every representation
V
in
\mathcal{C}
, there exists a unique discrete representation of
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
whose pull-back via
\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{C})

is
V
.

Proof.

Let
V\in\mathcal{C}
and denote by
f
the
R
-algebra
map from
A
to
{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)
associated to the
representation
V
. We regard
V
as a representation of
{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)
and we endow the latter algebra with the Tannakian topology generated by the unique representation
V
. Remark then that
{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)
is a separated topological
R
-algebra (see proposition
2.10
) and is moreover complete. Since
V
is in
\mathcal{C}
, note that the map
f
is continuous (see point 2 of proposition
2.11
). Therefore, and by definition of
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
, there exists a unique continuous
R
-algebra
morphism
\widehat{f}
from
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
to
{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{C})\,}
\textstyle{\,{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)\,.}
\textstyle{\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{f}}

A
R
-algebra morphism
g
from
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
to
{\mathrm{End}_{\!\!\;R}}(V)
being continuous if and only if the pull-back of
V
via
g
is a discrete representation of
\widehat{A}(\mathcal{C})
(see point 1 of proposition
2.11
), we are done.
∎

2.5
\psi
-integrable representations

In this subsection,
\psi
designates an
I
-colouring
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
.

Let
i\in I
, we denote by
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
the unique
R
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

to
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
which sends
X^{\pm}
and
H

to
X_{i}^{\pm}
and
\check{\alpha}_{i}
, respectively.

Definition 2.13
.

A representation
V
of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is said
\psi
-integrable
if the following two conditions are satisfied.

a)

The representation
V
is a weight representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
.

b)

For every
i\in I
, the pull-back of
V
via
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{i})}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Note, in view of condition b) in the previous definition, that the
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
are integrable and admit a character.

Definition 2.14
.

The
\psi
-integrable topology on
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is the Tannakian topology generated by the
\psi
-integrable representations of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
.

Note that
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, endowed with the
\psi
-integrable topology, is a topological
R
-algebra
, in view of point 1 of proposition
2.10
.

2.6
The algebra
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}

In this subsection,
\psi
designates an
I
-colouring
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
.

Definition 2.15
.

•

We denote by
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the (separated) completion of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
with respect to the
\psi
-integrable
topology.

•

If the
I
-colouring
\psi
is admissible, we call
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the coloured Kac-Moody algebra associated
to
\psi
.

By definition, the
R
-algebra
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is naturally
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed, we denote its
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-point
by
{\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

Remark that for every
\psi
-integrable
representation
V
of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, there is, in view of proposition
2.12
, a unique discrete representation of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
whose
pull-back
via
{\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}

is
V
. We regard from now
\psi
-integrable
representations of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
also as discrete representations of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. In the case
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
, the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

(
n\in\mathbb{N}
)
of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
are in particular regarded as discrete representations of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

Remark 2.16
.

•

In view of point 2 of proposition
2.10
, an element in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the
\psi
-integrable representations
of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
.

•

In particular, when
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
, an element in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is zero if and only if it acts as zero on all the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

(
n\in\mathbb{N}
)
.

We regard
Y
as a
\mathbb{Z}
-submodule
R^{X}
, thanks to the non-degenerate pairing between
Y
and
X
. We denote by
R[Y]
the
R
-subalgebra of
R^{X}
generated by
Y
.

Proposition 2.17
.

1)

Let
{(x_{a})}_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence with values in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
. For every
i\in I
, the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}x_{a}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{a}
converges to a unique element in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

2)

There exists a unique
R
-algebra morphism from
R^{X}
to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which sends every element
\check{\mu}
in
Y
to
\check{\mu}
in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

We regard from now every element in
R^{X}
as an element in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
as well.

3)

The image of
R[Y]
in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
is dense in the image of
R^{X}
.

4)

An element
f
in
R^{X}
acts as
f(\lambda)
on every weight vector of weight
\lambda
in a
\psi
-integrable
representation of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

Proof.

Remark that the sequence
{(x_{a}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{a}.v)}_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
is eventually zero, for every
vector
v
in a
\psi
-integrable
representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
. As a consequence, and by definition of the
\psi
-integrable
topology on
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, the partial sums associated to the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}x_{a}{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{a}
form a Cauchy sequence in
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
(see point 3 of proposition
2.10
). The latter series then converges to a unique element in the (separated) completion
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.

The
\mathbb{Z}
-module
X
being free, the pairing between
Y
and
X
being perfect, one can identify
X
with the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
R[Y]
with the
R
-subalgebra of
{R^{\!\>\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}
formed by the polynomials in
d
variables, where
d
designates the rank of the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
X
. Let now
f\in R^{X}
and let
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Remark that there exists a polynomial
{f_{\!\!\>N}}
in
R[Y]
such that the following holds:

f(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\ =\ {f_{\!\!\>N}}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\,,\quad\quad\forall\,k_{1},\dots,k_{d}\in\mathbb{Z},\ \,-N\leq k_{1},\dots,k_{d}\leq N\,.

Remark besides that
{f_{\!\!\>N}}
acts as
{f_{\!\!\>N}}(\lambda)
on every weight vector of weight
\lambda
in a
\psi
-integrable
representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
. Hence, and by definition of the
\psi
-integrable
topology on
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, the sequence
{({f_{\!\!\>N}})}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
(see again point 3 of proposition
2.10
). The sequence then converges to a unique element
\bar{f}
in the (separated) completion
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
.
∎

2.7
Congruence

Definition 2.18
.

•

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. The congruent class
[\psi]

of
\psi
is the function from
[E]
to
R
defined by the following:

[\psi](n,k)\ :=\ \psi^{-}(n,k)\ \psi^{+}(n,n-k+1)\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

•

Two colourings
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
are said congruent if
[\psi]=[\psi^{\prime}]
. We denote by
\equiv
the congruence relation on the set
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
.

The following proposition gives an interpretation of the congruence relation.

Proposition 2.19
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be two non-degenerate colourings of the
crystal
\mathbb{B}
with values
in
R
.

1)

If
\psi_{1}\equiv\psi_{2}
, then the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed

R
-algebras
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
are isomorphic.

2)

If
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
divide each other in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
, then the following two assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The colourings
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are congruent.

(ii)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

Let
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
be two non-degenerate colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
such that
\psi^{-}
divides
(\psi^{\prime})^{-}
in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. Let us first prove the following assertion.

The colourings
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are congruent if and only if there exists an injective
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(
\ast
)

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
b_{n,p}
is of weight vector of weigh
n-2p
in both
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, every
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
must send, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, the vector
b_{n,p}
to a scalar multiple of it. Since besides the vector
b_{n,p}
is a scalar multiple of
(X^{-})^{p}.b_{n,0}
in
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
, every
{U_{\!\!\>h}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
is thus a scalar multiple of the
R
-linear endomorphism
{f_{\!\!\>n}}
of
{RB_{n}}
, defined by the following:

f_{\!\!\>n}(b_{n,p})\ :=\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p)!}\ b_{n,p}\quad\quad\ (p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n})\,.

The colouring
\psi^{\prime}
being non-degenerate, the map
{f_{\!\!\>n}}
is injective. Remark then that for every
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}_{\leq n}
, the following equalities hold in
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}

and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
, respectively:

\displaystyle f_{\!\!\>n}\big(X^{+}.\!\>b_{n,p}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi^{+}(n,n-p+1)\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p-1)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p-1)!}\ b_{n,p}\,,

\displaystyle X^{+}.\big(f_{\!\!\>n}(b_{n,p})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi^{\prime})^{+}(n,n-p+1)\ \frac{(\psi^{\prime})^{-}(n,p)!}{\psi^{-}(n,p)!}\ b_{n,p}\,.

Assertion
(\ast)
follows.

Point 2 is a consequence. Let us now prove point 1. Denote by
\psi
the colouring of
\mathbb{B}
defined by the following:

\psi^{-}(n,k)\ :=\ 1\,,\quad\quad\psi^{+}(n,k)\ :=\ [\psi_{1}](n,k)\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

Remark that
\psi\equiv\psi_{1}
. Assertion
(\ast)
then implies that there exists an injective
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In view of the definition of the algebras
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
, there hence exists an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
R
-algebra between the two latter. One concludes.
∎

2.8
Diagram structure

In this subsection,
\psi
designates an
I
-colouring
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
.

Definition-proposition 2.20
.

Let
i\in I
. We denote by
{\widehat{\Delta}^{i}_{\!\!\;R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the unique continuous
R
-algebra morphism from
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ }
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}\ }
\textstyle{\,{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ {\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\Delta}^{i}_{\!\!\;R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{\,{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\,.}

Proof.

The pull-back via
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
of a
\psi
-integrable
representation of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
being by definition
\psi_{i}
-integrable, the
R
-algebra morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is continuous with respect to the
\psi_{i}
-integrable topology on
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
and to the
\psi
-integrable topology
on
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
(see
point 2
of proposition
2.11
). The algebras
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
and
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
being the (separated) completions of the
R
-algebras
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

and
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
, respectively, one concludes.
∎

We regard every element in
Y
as an element in
\mathbb{Z}^{X}
as well, thanks to the pairing between
Y
and
X
.

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
i\in I
. The morphism
{\widehat{\Delta}^{i}_{\!\!\;R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
sends every element
f
in
R^{\mathbb{Z}}
to the element
f\circ\check{\alpha}_{i}
in
R^{X}
.

Proof.

The morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
sends by definition
H
to
\check{\alpha}_{i}
. Recall besides that we regard
Y
, and in particular
\mathbb{Z}
, as
\mathbb{Z}
-submodules of
R^{X}
and
R^{\mathbb{Z}}
, respectively, in a such a way that
H
is identified with the ring morphism from
\mathbb{Z}
to
R
. Remark then that the morphism
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
sends
f
to
f\circ\check{\alpha}_{i}
, for every
f
lying in the
R
-subalgebra
R[H]
of
R^{\mathbb{Z}}
generated by
H
. The image of
R[H]
in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{i})}
being dense in the image of
R^{\mathbb{Z}}
(see point 3 of proposition
2.17
), one concludes.
∎

2.9
Cartan involution

We denote by
{\omega_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
is the involutive
R
-algebra automorphism of
{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}
which sends
X_{i}^{\pm}
(
i\in I
) and
\check{\mu}\in Y
to
-X_{i}^{\mp}
and
-\check{\mu}
, respectively. Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{Col}^{I}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
, we denote by
\psi^{\ast}
the
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
defined by the following:

{(\psi^{\ast})}^{\pm}_{i}\ :=\ \psi_{i}^{\mp}\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

Definition-proposition 2.22
.

We denote by
{\widehat{\omega}_{\!\!\;R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}
the unique continuous
R
-algebra isomorphism from
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi^{\ast})}
which makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\omega_{\!\!\;R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ }
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\ }
\textstyle{\,{U_{\!R}({\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ {\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi^{\ast})}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\omega}_{\!\!\;R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi)}}
\textstyle{\,{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{g},\psi^{\ast})}\,.}

3
Classical and quantum realisations

3.1
Standard quantum algebras

Let
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and let
i\in I
. The following elements are defined in
\Bbbk[q^{\pm 1}]
:

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle q^{d_{i}}\,,

[k]_q_i

:=

q

i

k

- q

i

-k

q

i

- q

i

-1

,

\displaystyle{[k]}_{q_{i}}!

\displaystyle\ :=\

\displaystyle\prod_{k^{\prime}=0}^{k}\,{[k^{\prime}]}_{q_{i}}\ ,

[

k

k’

]
_q_i

:=

[k+k’]

q

i

!

[k]

q

i

! [k-k’]

q

i

!

⋅

The
standard (rational) quantum algebra

{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{g})
is the
\Bbbk(q)
-algebra generated by the elements
X^{-}_{i},X^{+}_{i},K_{\check{\mu}}
(
i\in I,\check{\mu}\in Y
), and subject to the following relations:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rclr}K_{0}&=&1&\\
K_{\check{\mu}}\!\;K_{\check{\mu}^{\prime}}&=&K_{\check{\mu}+\check{\mu}^{\prime}}&(\check{\mu},\check{\mu}^{\prime}\in Y)\,,\\
K_{\check{\mu}}\!\>X^{\pm}_{i}&=&q^{\pm\langle{\check{\mu}},\alpha_{i}\rangle}\!\;X_{i}^{\pm}\!\;K_{\check{\mu}}&(i\in I,\ \check{\mu}\in Y)\,,\\
\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{j}\big]&=&0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle{\big[X^{+}_{i},X^{-}_{i}\big]}=\displaystyle{\frac{K_{i}-K_{i}^{-1}}{q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1}}}\,,\quad\quad\text{where }\,K_{i}:=K_{d_{i}\check{\alpha}_{i}}&(i\in I)\,,\\
\displaystyle{\sum_{k+k^{\prime}=1-C_{ij}}(-1)^{k}\,{1-C_{ij}\brack k}_{q_{i}}\,{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k}\;\!X^{\pm}_{j}\;\!{(X_{i}^{\pm})}^{k^{\prime}}}=\,\displaystyle 0&\quad\quad(i,j\in I,\ i\neq j)\,.\end{array}}\right.

3.2
Classical and quantum colourings

The
classical colouring

\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
is the colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk
defined by the following:

{\psi_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}^{\pm}}(n,k)\ :=\ k\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

We denote by
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
the
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk
defined by the following:

{({\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}_{i}\ :=\ \psi_{\mathrm{cl}}\quad\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

Note that
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
is admissible over
\Bbbk
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The
(n+1)
-dimensional irreducible representation (of type 1)
{L_{q}}(n)
of the quantum algebra
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
can be defined as the
\mathbb{C}(q)
-vector space
{\Bbbk(q)B_{n}}
, where the actions of
X^{\pm}
,
K
(
={K_{\!\!\>H}}
) are given by the following, for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\leq n}
:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\ (n-2p)\,b_{n,p}\,,\\
&&\\
X^{-}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[p+1]}_{q}\,b_{n,p+1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq n$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else,}\end{array}}\right.\\
&&\\
X^{+}.\!\;b_{n,p}&:=&\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cl}{[n-p+1]}_{q}\,b_{n,p-1}&\quad\text{if $p\neq 0$,}\\
0&\quad\text{else.}\end{array}}\right.\end{array}}\right.

The
quantum colouring

{\psi_{\!\!\;q}}
is the
colouring
of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[q^{\pm 1}]
, defined by the following:

{\psi_{\!\!\;q}}^{\pm}(n,k)\ :=\ {[k]}_{q}\quad\quad\quad(n,k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast},\ k\leq n)\,.

We besides denote by
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
the
I
-colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
\Bbbk[q^{\pm 1}]
defined by the following:

{({\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}})}_{i}\ :=\ \psi_{q_{i}}\quad\quad(i\in I)\,.

Note that
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}}
is admissible over
\Bbbk(q)
.

3.3
Theorem

We regard in the following theorem
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
as a
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-algebra, endowed with the Tannakian topology generated by the integrable representations of
\mathfrak{g}
, and endowed with the projection map from
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F})}
to
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
.

We besides denote by
K_{\check{\mu}}
(
\check{\mu}\in Y
) the function in
{\Bbbk(q)^{X}\!\subset{\widehat{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk(q)}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}})}}
whose value at
\lambda\in X
is
q^{\langle\check{\mu},\lambda\rangle}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

1)

•

The coloured Kac-Moody algebra
{\widehat{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}
contains
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
as a dense
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
-pointed
\Bbbk
-subalgebra.

•

The action of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
on every integrable representation can be uniquely extended to an integrable and discrete action of
{\widehat{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}
.

2)

•

The coloured Kac-Moody algebra
{\widehat{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk(q)}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\;q}})}
contains
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{g})
as a dense
\Bbbk(q)
-subalgebra, in such a way that the elements
X_{i}^{\pm},K_{\check{\mu}}
(
i\in I,\check{\mu}\in Y
) of the two algebras are identified.

•

The action of
{U_{\!\!\>q}}(\mathfrak{g})
on every integrable representation can be uniquely extended to an integrable and discrete action of
{\widehat{U}_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g},{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}})}
.

Proof.

Let
V
be an integrable representation of
\mathfrak{g}
and let
i\in I
. Remark that the pull-back of
V
via
{\Delta^{i}_{\!\>\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{g})}
is an integrable representation of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
and is then a direct sum of representations
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
(see
subsection
1.5
). Remark besides that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n)}
, when viewed as a representation of
{U_{\!\!\;\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
, is by definition of the classical colouring
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
equal to the representation
{L_{\Bbbk}(n,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
(see subsection
2.3
). Hence, the
representation
V
, when viewed as a representation of
{\mathfrak{g}_{\!\!\;F}}
, is
{\psi^{I}_{\!\!\>\mathrm{cl}}}
-integrable.
Point 1
of the theorem follows. The proof in the quantum case is similar.
∎

4
Main results for coloured Kac-Moody algebras of rank 1

4.1
H
-triviality

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be two admissible colourings of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
. There exists a
R
-algebra isomorphism from
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
which fixes the
elements
X^{-},H
and such that the pull-back of
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}
via
f
is isomorphic
to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
two congruent colourings which divide each other in the ring
{\mathrm{Col}_{\!\!\>R}\!\!\;(\mathbb{B})}
. We know that the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi^{\prime})}
are isomorphic: see proposition
2.19
. We also know that
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
and
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi^{\prime})}
are isomorphic
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed algebras: see again proposition
2.19
. Therefore, and since the colourings
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
are admissible, one can without loss of generality assume that
\psi_{1}^{-}=\psi^{-}_{2}=1
.

Since
\psi_{2}
is invertible, there exists a sequence
{(M_{a})}_{a\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
R^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
M[1]
is solution of the equation
{\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\!\;[0]}_{\!\!\>R}(\psi_{2},\psi_{1})}
: see proposition
A.3
. Denote then by
g
the
R
-algebra morphism from
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}

to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
defined by the following:

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{lcl}g(X^{-})&:=&X^{-}\,,\\
g(H)&:=&H\,,\\
g(X^{+})&:=&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}\,.\end{array}}\right.

Note that the series
\sum_{a\in\mathbb{N}}{(X^{-})}^{a}M_{a}(H){(X^{+})}^{a+1}
converges in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
: see point 1 of proposition
2.17
. Remark that for every
n,p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0<p\leq n
, the following two equalities hold in the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
and
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}
, respectively:

\displaystyle X^{+}.\;\!b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\psi_{1}](n,p)\ b_{n,p-1}\,,

\displaystyle g(X^{+}).\;\!b_{n,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq p-1}\,M_{a}(n-2p+2a+2)\ \frac{[\psi_{2}](n,p)!}{[\psi_{2}](n,p-a-1)!}\ b_{n,p-1}\,.

Since
g
fixes
X^{-},H
and since the column matrix
M[1]
is a solution of the equation
{\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\!\;[0]}_{\!\!\>R}(\psi_{2},\psi_{1})}
, the pull-back of
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}
via
g
is in consequence equal to the representation
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular, the pull-back via
g
of every
\psi_{2}
-integrable representation of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
is a
\psi_{1}
-integrable
representation of
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
, and the map
g
is thus continuous, where
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
is endowed with the
\psi_{1}
-integrable topology (see point 5 of proposition
2.10
). By definition of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
, and since
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
is separated complete, there exists a
R
-algebra morphism
f
from the former to the latter which makes the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\pi}_{\!\!\>R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}\ }
\textstyle{\,{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}\,.}
\textstyle{{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}

Remark then that
f
, as
g
, fixes
X^{-},H
and that the pull-back of
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}

via
f
is equal to the representation
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

By inverting the roles of
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, there also exists a
R
-algebra morphism
f^{\prime}
from
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
to
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
, which fixes
X^{-},H
and such that the pull-back of
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}

via
f^{\prime}
is equal to the representation
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{2})}
of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the actions of
(f^{\prime}\circ f)(x)
and
x
on
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{1})}
are equal for every
x\in{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. As a consequence, the map
f^{\prime}\circ f
is equal to the identify map on
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{1})}
(see remark
2.16
). One proves in the same way that
f\circ f^{\prime}
is the identity map on
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{2})}
.
∎

4.2
Integrable representations

Let
\psi
be a colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values in
R
. We denote by
{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
the category of integrable and discrete representations of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
which are free when viewed as
R
-modules.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
\psi
be an admissible colouring of
\mathbb{B}
with values
in
R
. We assume that
R
is a principal ideal domain.

1)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the representation
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is topologically generated by a vector
v_{n}
and subject to the following relations:

H.\!\>v_{n}\ =\ n\;\!v_{n}\,,\quad\quad X^{+}.\!\>v_{n}\ =\ 0\,,\quad\quad(X^{-})^{n+1}.\!\>v\ =\ 0\,.

2)

The representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) are pairwise distinct and are the unique, up to isomorphism, indecomposable representations in
{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
.

3)

A representation of
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
is in the category
{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi)}
if and only if it is
\psi
-integrable and discrete.

4)

Let
n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. Every
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F})}
-morphism from
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi)}
to
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n^{\prime},\psi)}
is equal to a scalar multiple of the identity map if
n=n^{\prime}
and is zero else.

Proof.

Let us first prove points 2 and 3 of the theorem. Since
\psi
and the classical colouring
\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
are invertible over
R
, one can assume without loss of generality, in view of theorem
4.1
,
that
\psi=\psi_{\mathrm{cl}}
. We regard
{U_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}
as a
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
R
-algebra
in a natural way. The relation
[X^{-},X^{+}]=H
being satisfied in all the representations
{L_{\!\!\>R}(n,\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
), it is also
in
{\widehat{U}_{\!R}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2},\psi_{\mathrm{cl}})}
(see
remark
2.16
). As a consequence, there exists a
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,F}
-pointed
R
